Coadministration of enalapril with aprocitentan decreased the MAP beyond the predicted additive value (↓12±3, ↓14±2, and ↓26±4 mmHg in enalapril, aprocitentan and combination of both, respectively) demonstrating a synergism between the two compounds.
Coadministration of enalapril with spironolactone decreased the MAP to the predicted additive value (↓17±4, ↓12±4, and ↓27±4 mmHg in enalapril, spironolactone and combination of both, respectively) of both compounds.
Except for a moderate HR increase in enalapril-treated rats, no change in HR was observed in the other treatment groups.

L722: The effects of various treatment combinations with aprocitentan on blood pressure are summarized in the table below. In DOCA-salt rats, coadministration of aprocitentan and valsartan decreased MAP beyond a value predicted by an assumption of additive behavior, demonstrating synergism between the two compounds. In contrast, spironolactone combined with valsartan produced a purely additive effect. Coadministration of aprocitentan with enalapril demonstrated synergy in SHR but simple additive effects in DOCA-salt rats. Aprocitentan and amlodipine combination produced additive effects in SHR and DOCA-salt rats. Overall, these combination studies demonstrated that the addition of aprocitentan may be anticipated to have an additive to synergistic impact on blood pressure (BP) reduction when coadministered with other antihypertensive drugs.

L729: ## 13.1.1.2. Secondary Pharmacology
L730: In an off-target screening assessment (Study No: T-15.025), the potential for interaction with 53 known receptor systems or enzymes was assessed. Aprocitentan (10µM) did not produce any noteworthy effects (defined as &gt;50% inhibition or stimulation) in any of these assays.
L731: ## 13.1.1.3. Safety Pharmacology
L732: A battery of safety pharmacology assessments (central nervous system, cardiovascular, and respiratory endpoints) was conducted, and no significant safety concerns were identified. Decreased BP and a compensatory increase in heart rate, which is considered related to the
L733: desired primary pharmacological effect of aprocitentan, were observed. Results of these studies are summarized in the table below.
L734: **Table 46. Safety Pharmacology Studies**

Cells/strain: Human embryonic kidney cells (HEK293)/stably expressed recombinant hERG channels (hERG/Ikr)
Concentration: 3, 10, 30 and 100µM	hERG tail current amplitude was reduced by more than 50% at 30µM and 100µM.
The IC50 and IC20 values were determined as 28.6µM and 7.2µM, respectively.

L737: ## 13.1.2. Pharmacokinetics/ADME/Toxicokinetics
L738: General pharmacokinetic (PK) assessments, including absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion (ADME), and toxicokinetic evaluations with aprocitentan were conducted in rodents (rats) and nonrodents (rabbits and dogs) following oral (PO) or intravenous (IV) administration. The species and strains were those used in the toxicology program. Overall, the animal data predicted the PK/ADME profile in humans and no significant limitations on the interpretability of animal toxicology studies for predicting human safety were identified.
L739: ## Absorption
L740: Single-dose PK studies were conducted in male rats (0.5 mg/kg, IV or 3 mg/kg, PO) (B-05.130) and dogs (0.3 mg/kg, IV or 3 mg, PO) (B-13.249) by administering the (b) (4) form of aprocitentan. PK parameters are summarized in the tables below. Aprocitentan was identified as a low-clearance drug in rats (1.3 mL/min\cdotkg) and dogs (0.4 mL/min\cdotkg). In rats, oral absorption was slow with a median time to maximum concentration (T_{max}) of 8 h and oral bioavailability was 41% ((b) (4) form in suspension). The slow uptake observed in this assessment was likely due to poor solubility and/or slow dissolution of the drug substance. In dogs when administered with a more clinically relevant (b) (4) aprocitentan, oral bioavailability was ~90% and the T_{max} was 3 hours.

L766: ## **Distribution**
L767: The volume of distribution (Vd) of aprocitentan was determined in rats (0.5 mg/kg, IV) (B-05.130) and dogs (0.3 mg/kg, IV) (B-13.249). The mean Vdss was 1.0 L/kg in the rat and 0.3 L/kg in the dog, which approximates total body water volume.
L768: The tissue distribution of aprocitentan (0.3 mg/kg, P.O., 14C-radiolabel) was evaluated in male albino Wistar rats (for 10 days) and pigmented Lister Hooded rats (for 15 days) using quantitative whole-body autoradiography (B-14.029). Absorption and distribution of radioactivity into tissues were similar in both strains. The liver, kidney cortex, lung, pituitary, thyroid, and myocardium showed the highest exposures, with peak exposures observed at 8 hours postdose. No relevant retention of radioactivity in the uveal tract/retina, pigmented skin, or meninges of the brain was observed at any time point.
L769: Binding of aprocitentan (14C-radiolabel, 0.1–300 \mug/mL) to plasma proteins was measured in mouse, rat, rabbit, dog, and human plasma using equilibrium dialysis (B-07.077). High plasma protein binding (&gt;98%) was observed in all species, with a free fraction between 0.1% to 0.5% (Table 51).
L770: **Table 51. Plasma Protein Binding of Aprocitentan in Different Species**
L771: Species	Fraction bound (%)	Free fraction (%)
Mouse	99.0	1.0
Rat	98.7	1.3
Rabbit	99.9	0.1
Dog	98.3	1.7
Human	99.5	0.5
L772: Source: Applicant-generated table (Table 6) from Pharmacokinetics Written Summary.
L773: Partitioning into red blood cells was assessed in Study B-07.077. Mean blood/plasma ratios were observed in the range of 0.54 to 0.69 for all species, indicating that aprocitentan has limited ability to partition into red blood cells.
L774: ## Metabolism
L775: Metabolite structure was evaluated in vitro using rat and human hepatocytes, bile using bile duct-cannulated rats, and in urine. Aprocitentan was heavily metabolized along five different pathways, producing a total of 15 metabolites that were characterized, as shown in Figure 14.

L791: All human metabolites were detected in the rat and/or the dog. No disproportionate human metabolites were identified.
L792: In the repeated dose rat and dog toxicity studies, aprocitentan increased the mRNA expression of cyp3a1, cyp2b1, cyp2b2, cyp2b2, ugt1a1, ugt1a6 and ugt2b1 (B-17.046) in rats and cyp2A12 mRNA in dogs (B-17.047) in a dose-dependent manner, indicating a species-specific enzyme induction in liver.
L793: ## Excretion
L794: The major excretion pathway for aprocitentan in rats was identified as biliary based on a study in bile duct-cannulated rats. In this study, oral (3 mg) or intravenous (1 mg) administration of aprocitentan (^{14}\text{C}-labeled ACT-132577B) accounted for 50% of total oral dose and 70% of total intravenous dose recovered in bile. Renal excretion represented 16% and 14% of total dose for the oral and intravenous routes, respectively. Approximately 30% of the total oral dose and 22% of total intravenous dose was recovered in feces (B-14.034). At 72 hours, the mean total recovery was approximately 90% of the total drug exposure for the oral route and complete elimination of the drug was measured following intravenous administration.
L795: No milk excretion studies were conducted with aprocitentan; however, aprocitentan was consistently observed in milk samples across all time points after oral administration of ^{14}\text{C}-labeled macitentan (B-11.837 Annex) in rats.
L796: ## Toxicokinetics
L797: In repeat-dose oral studies, systemic exposure generally increased in a dose-dependent manner in both rats and dogs. The exposure was decreased at the end of the dosing period in rats compared to Day 1. The toxicokinetic parameters in the pivotal toxicology studies are summarized in the table below.

L868: ## 13.1.3. Toxicology
L869: ## 13.1.3.1. General Toxicology
L870: Repeat dose toxicology studies were conducted in rats (up to 26 weeks) and dogs (up to 39 weeks). These animal species were considered relevant for human risk assessment because pharmacodynamic (PD) and PK/ADME profiles are similar to those in humans. Key results are summarized in the following subsections.
L871: aprocitentan (ACT132577) was administered in all repeated-dose toxicity studies in dogs, whereas aprocitentan (ACT-132577E) was administered in all rat general toxicity studies (except for a 2-week dose range finding study). The form (ACT132577E) produced approximately 6-fold higher exposure than to the at the same dose level in rats.
L872: ## 13.1.3.1.1. Repeat-Dose Toxicity Studies in Rats
L873: Repeat-dose toxicity studies were conducted in rats for up to 26-weeks with oral dosing of 10 to 1000 mg/kg/day aprocitentan. Two 26-week toxicity studies were conducted with the aprocitentan form (10 to 250 mg/kg/day). The first study was conducted in Wistar rats. To further evaluate the thyroidal follicular hyperplasia and adenomas observation in the first study, a supplemental study was conducted only in male Wistar and Sprague Dawley (SD) rats. Key findings from each 26-week study are summarized in the table below.
L874: ## 26-Week (9-Week Recovery) Toxicology Studies
L875: ## **Study Number/Title: 8310210/ACT-132577E: 26 Week Oral (Gavage) Administration Toxicity Study in the Rat Followed by a 9 Week Treatment-Free Period**
L876: ## Key Study Finding
L877: - Dose dependent increases in the incidence and severity of dark and enlarged liver was observed with an associated increase in organ weights due to hepatocellular hypertrophy. These changes were due to reversible microsomal enzyme induction, considered an adaptive response.
- At the highest dose of 250 mg/kg/day, adverse focal to multifocal centrilobular hydropic degeneration of hepatocytes were observed.
- Reversible follicular hypertrophy at \ge 10 mg/kg/day and hyperplasia at \ge 50 mg/kg/day in the thyroid glands were considered adaptive response as a consequence of increased hepatic metabolism. Follicular adenomas observed at doses \ge 100 mg/kg/day in this study was not observed in the supplemental study at doses up to 250 mg/kg/day in male SD and Wistar rats for the same duration. The findings in the thyroid were not considered clinically relevant.
- The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) was determined to be 50 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-24h} values of 619 h\cdot\mug/mL, approximately 18-fold the AUC at the maximum recommended human dose [MRHD]), based on clinically irrelevant thyroid follicular adenomas at \ge 100 mg/kg/day and multifocal hydropic degeneration of hepatocytes at 250 mg/kg/day.

L884: **Table 55. Observations and Results for Study Number 8310210/ACT-132577E**
L885: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	One female animal in control group due to multiple clinical signs including abnormal respiration, protruding eyes and weight loss, and one male animal in 250 mg/kg/day group due to impaired mobility were sacrificed early. As no other animals showed a similar clinical observation at 250 mg/kg/day, the impaired mobility was not considered treatment related.
Clinical signs	Minor findings in the treatment groups were not considered adverse effects.
Body weights	Slight decrease in mean body weight gain was noted (-8.0% in male and -19% in female) at 250 mg/kg/day and no differences were noted during the recovery phase.
Ophthalmoscopy	No treatment-related adverse findings were observed.
Hematology	No treatment related changes were noted except a slight reduction in RBC, Hb and PCV (-7%).
Clinical chemistry	Dose-dependent increase in total protein in all the doses tested, with associated increase in globulin \ge 100 mg/kg/day was observed in female animals. Cholesterol was increased in females at \ge 100 mg/kg/day, and alanine aminotransferase was increased in males at 250 mg/kg/day. These changes were reversed at the 9-week recovery phase.
Gross pathology	Liver: Dose-dependent increase in dark and enlarged liver was noted at all the doses tested with increased severity with dose. It was not recovered in two females at 250 mg/kg/day.
Kidney: Dose-dependent increase in dark kidney was mainly observed in female (except one male at 250 mg/kg/day) animals. It was not recovered in one female at 10 mg/kg/day and two females at 100 mg/kg/day and three females at 250 mg/kg/day.
Thyroid glands: Dose dependent enlarged lobes were observed in thyroids.
Organ weights	Reversible dose dependent increase in organ absolute and/or relative weights (body weight and brain weight) were observed in liver (males \ge 10 mg/kg/day and females \ge 10 mg/kg/day), thyroid gland (\ge 100 mg/kg/day) and kidney (\ge 100 mg/kg/day).
L886: Parameters	Major Findings
Histopathology
Adequate battery:
Yes	
Liver: Dose-dependent increased incidence and/or severity of hepatocellular hypertrophy (centrilobular to diffuse, minimal to marked) was observed. These changes were fully reversible after the recovery period and were likely related to enzyme induction, hence, were considered adaptive response rather than adverse effects. At 250 mg/kg/day, a focal-to-multifocal hydropic degeneration of hepatocytes, mainly at a centrilobular location was observed, which was fully resolved at the end of recovery. The affected hepatocytes appeared swollen/balloon with clear cytoplasm occasionally associated with increased apoptosis.
Kidney: Brownish pigment was observed (males \ge 50 mg/kg/day and females \ge 10 mg/kg/day) in the tubular epithelia (localized mainly at proximal tubules in the outer cortex) with more incidences in females and it is not fully reversed.
Thyroid glands: Follicular hypertrophy (males at \ge 10 mg/kg/day and in females at \ge 50 mg/kg/day) was observed. Follicular hyperplasia (distributed mainly diffusely, rarely focally as nodular hyperplasia) was observed in males and females at \ge 50 mg/kg/day. These reversible changes were considered adaptive response to increased metabolism (increased hepatic glucuronosyltransferases (ugt1a1, 1a6, 2b1), which are responsible for metabolism of thyroid hormones) and excretion of thyroid hormones and not considered significant test article related adverse effects. Follicular adenomas were observed in two males at 100 mg/kg/day, three males, and one female at 250 mg/kg/day. These lesions were partially resolved in the recovery period.
Testes: No effect on testes was reported. Minor observations in 1 or 2 animals at 10 mg/kg/day and at 250 mg/kg/day were considered sporadic due to lack of any dose responsiveness.

L889: To further evaluate the occurrence of thyroidal follicular hyperplasia and adenomas in this 26-week rat study, a supplemental 26-week study (T-16.021) was performed with a detailed thyroid functional and histopathological evaluation in male Wistar and SD rats. This study is reviewed below.
L890: ## **Study Number/Title: T-16.021/ACT-132577E – 26-Week Toxicity Study by the Oral Route (Gavage) in the Male Rat Followed by a 9-Week Treatment-Free Period**
L891: ## Key Study Finding
L892: Adverse histopathology findings:
L893: - A dose-dependent increase in centrilobular hepatocellular hypertrophy accompanied by increased centrilobular hepatocellular fatty change was observed in liver. At 250 mg/kg/day a moderate hepatocellular vacuolar degeneration/necrosis of hypertrophic hepatocytes was observed.
- Reversible increases in thyroid-stimulating hormone were noted in Wistar rats (250 mg/kg/day) and SD rats (all the dose levels tested), establishing the cause of thyroid follicular cell hyperplasia and adenomas observed in the original 26-week rat study.
L894: - Reversible findings in the thyroid gland included a dose-dependent minimal to slight follicular cell hypertrophy with correlating enlarged thyroid and increased weight at \ge 50 mg/kg/day and minimal or slight focal follicular cell hyperplasia in two SD rats treated with 250 mg/kg/day. No follicular adenomas were observed in this study.
- Minimal dilation of seminiferous tubules (50 or 250 mg/kg/day) and increased incidence of tubular degeneration/atrophy was observed in testes at 250 mg/kg/day which were resolved completely at the end of recovery.
- The NOAEL was determined to be 50 mg/kg/day, based on liver necrosis and testicular tubular degeneration/atrophy observed at 250 mg/kg/day. The NOAEL exposures represented AUC_{0-\text{last}} values of 554 h\cdot\mug/mL in Wistar rats and 474 h\cdot\mug/mL in SD rats, or approximately 16 and 14-fold the AUC at MRHD, respectively.

L899: **Table 57. Observations and Results for Study Number T-16.021/ACT-132577E**
L900: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	
In the Wistar rats' group, five animals were either found dead (control (n=1) on Day 154, 10 mg/kg/day (n=1) on Day 151, and 50 mg/kg/day (n=1) on Day 149) or sacrificed early due to moribund condition (50 mg/kg/day (n=2) on Day 95 and 179).
In the SD rats' group, four animals were either found dead (control (n=1) on Day 74) or sacrificed early due to moribund condition (control (n=1) on Day 171 and 250 mg/kg/day (n=1) on Day 134).
The cause of death was identified as misintubation for the animals that were found dead and the animals that were found moribund were not identified as treatment-related. Therefore, it was unlikely that these early deaths could be attributed to the treatment with test article.

## TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
L902: Parameters	Major Findings
Clinical signs	Minor findings in the treatment groups were not considered adverse effects.
Body weights	Decrease in mean body weight was observed in both of the species (-12% in Wistar rats and -11% in SD rats from Day 182 compared to respective control values) at 250 mg/kg/day and less pronounced during the recovery phase.
Ophthalmoscopy	Incidental findings were not considered treatment-related.
Hematology	No treatment related changes were observed except a slight reduction in RBC, Hb and PCV (-7% maximum)
Clinical chemistry	Increases in total protein with associated increases in globulin levels were observed in Wistar and SD rats at 250 mg/kg/day. Lower triglyceride levels (-74% in Wistar rats and -52% in SD rats at Week 13 and -65% in Wistar rats and -62% in SD rats at Week 26) were noted at 250 mg/kg/day. These changes were reversible at the 9-week recovery phase.

Enlarged thyroid glands were observed at 250 mg/kg/day in both Wistar rats and SD rats.

Histopathology	Liver: In both strains a dose-dependent centrilobular hepatocellular hypertrophy accompanied by increased centrilobular fatty change was observed in all the treated animal groups. A moderate hepatocellular vacuolar degeneration/necrosis in the hypertrophic hepatocytes (several animals at 250 mg/kg/day) with minimal multinucleated hepatocytes in five animals was observed. These findings were either fully (hepatocellular degeneration/necrosis and multinucleated hepatocytes) or partially (hepatocellular hypertrophy and fatty changes) reversed at the end of the recovery phase.
Adequate battery: Yes	Thyroid glands: A dose-dependent follicular cell hypertrophy was recorded at minimal and/or slight severity degree of all treated groups which is accompanied by minimal or slight focal follicular cell hyperplasia in two SD rats treated with 250 mg/kg/day. These findings were fully reversed at the end of the recovery phase.
Testes: Minimal dilation of seminiferous tubules of the testes of both strains was observed at 50 or 250 mg/kg/day, and an increased incidence of tubular degeneration/atrophy at 250 mg/kg/day. Both findings showed complete reversibility after the recovery period. There was no finding of the testes reported in another 26-week rat study at the same dose levels. The dilation of seminiferous tubules only observed at the 50 mg/kg/day was of minimal severity and low incidence, hence, was not considered adverse.

L905: ## 13.1.3.1.2. Repeat Dose Toxicity Studies in Dogs
L906: Repeat dose toxicity studies were conducted in dogs for up to 39 weeks with oral dose levels from 5 to 1000 mg/kg/day. Key findings are summarized below.
L907: ## 39-Week (13-Week Recovery) Toxicology Study
L908: **Study Number/Title:** T-15.053/ACT-132577: 39 Week Oral (Capsule) Administration Toxicity Study in the Dog Followed by a 13 Week Treatment-Free Period
L909: ## Key Study Findings
L910: - Dose related occurrence of noisy respiration was observed at \ge 25 mg/kg/day.
- Clinical signs, including hunched posture, pale or thin appearance, and subdued/sluggish behavior were observed at the high dose (75 mg/kg/day), leading to its reduction to 50 mg/kg/day starting from Day 163 in males and Day 165 in females.
- Testes: minimal to slight tubular degeneration at doses \ge 25 mg/kg/day.
- Enlarged liver lobes and/or mottled livers were observed (males at 25 mg/kg/day and both sexes at 75/50 mg/kg/day) with associated increased weights and hepatocellular hypertrophy at \ge 25 mg/kg/day. These changes were fully resolved at the end of recovery.
- The NOAEL was determined at 5 mg/kg/day, based on noisy respiration, tubular degeneration in the testes at higher doses. The NOAEL was associated with AUC0-24hr values of 374 h*\mug/mL, approximately 11-fold the exposure at MRHD.

L922: ## 13.1.3.2. Genotoxicity Studies
L923: Aprocitentan showed no mutagenic or clastogenic activity in a standard battery of genotoxicity studies, which includes a bacterial reverse mutation study, an in vitro chromosomal aberration study in human lymphocytes, and an in vivo micronucleus study in rats.
L924: **Table 60. Genetic Toxicology**
L925: Study/Study Number	Key Study Findings
Study no: T-14.025
Study title: ACT-132577: Salmonella typhimurium Reverse-Mutation Assay
Doses tested: 3, 10, 33, 100, 333, 1000, 2500, 5000 µg/plate for Salmonella strains TA 1535, TA 1537, TA 98, TA 100, and TA 102
GLP compliance: Yes
Study is valid: Yes	Aprocitentan did not increase the number of revertants in any of the strains tested in the presence or absence of metabolic activation; therefore, aprocitentan was considered negative for mutagenicity in this bacterial reverse mutation assay.
Positive controls demonstrated expected S9- and strain-dependent increases in revertant colonies.
Study no: T-14.026
Study title: Evaluation of the Ability of ACT-132577 to Induce Chromosome Aberrations in Cultured Peripheral Human Lymphocytes
Doses tested: Experiment 1: Up to 500 µg/mL for a 3 h exposure time with a 24 h fixation time in the absence and presence of S9 mix
Experiment 2: Up to 75 µg/mL for a 24 h continuous exposure time with a 24 h fixation time in the absence of S9 mix
GLP compliance: Yes
Study is valid: Yes	Aprocitentan did not induce any increase in the number of cells with chromosome aberrations and did not have an effect on the number of polyploid cells or cells with endoreduplicated chromosomes in the absence and presence of S9 mix.
Positive control chemicals induced a statistically significant increase in the incidence of cells with chromosome aberrations.
Aprocitentan is not considered clastogenic in human lymphocytes.
L926: Study/Study Number	Key Study Findings
Study No: T-15.006

Species/strain: Rat/Han Wistar
Number/sex/group: 6/sex/group
Doses tested: 100, 500 and 1400 mg/kg/day
Route and duration: Oral, daily on Day 1 and Day 2
GLP compliance: Yes	No mortality, drug related clinical signs or effect on body weights were observed. Animals were systemically exposed to ACT-132577E.
Male animals showed significant heterogeneity in MN-PCE frequencies at \ge 500 mg/kg/day. One animal had shown to have a MN-PCE frequency that exceeded the laboratory's historical control ranges in 1400 mg/kg/day and led to nonsignificant increase in the group mean frequency of MN-PCE.
Two female animals (one each at 100 and 500 mg/kg/day) frequencies of MN-PCE were that exceeded the laboratory's historical control ranges. No significant increase in the group mean frequency of MN-PCE were observed.
Overall, ACT-132577E treatment did not alter %PCE and MN-PCE frequencies; therefore, it is considered not induce micronuclei in the polychromatic erythrocytes of the bone marrow.
L927: Source: Pharmacology/toxicology reviewer generated table base on Study Reports T-14.025, T-14.026, and T-15.006
Abbreviations: GLP, good laboratory practices, h, hour; MN-PCE, micronucleated polychromatic erythrocyte, no., number; PCE, polychromatic erythrocyte; S9, supernatant at 9000 g; TA, Tester strain (Ames test)
L928: ## 13.1.3.3. Carcinogenicity Studies
L929: No dedicated carcinogenicity studies were conducted with apocitentan. Aprocitentan is a major metabolite of macitentan, and its exposure represented \ge 38\% of the total exposure to macitentan and apocitentan in the macitentan carcinogenicity studies. Therefore, the macitentan carcinogenicity studies, conducted in B6C3F1 mice and Wistar rats, were considered acceptable to assess the carcinogenic potential of apocitentan. The studies in mice and rats were conducted in accordance with good laboratory practice (GLP) regulations and prior concurrence with the CDER's Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (CAC) was obtained regarding the strain, number of animals employed, and doses evaluated. Both studies were reviewed previously in NDA 204410 and were concluded to be negative, as described in the label of macitentan. Below is a brief summary of the results of those studies based on previous review.
L930: ## 13.1.3.3.1. T-09.160: ACT-064992: 104-Week Oncogenicity (Gavage) Study in the B6C3F1 Mouse
L931: Male and female B6C3F1 mice (60 mice/sex/group for main study and 24 mice/sex/group for toxicokinetic evaluation) were treated with macitentan (vehicle; 0.5% methylcellulose in purified water, 5, 30, 100, and 400 mg/kg/day, oral) for a duration of 104 weeks, except for females of the 400 mg/kg dose group. Due to high morbidity/mortality in females (35/60), the 400 mg/kg/day group was led to early termination at Week 79. The cause of death could not be established in most animals.
L932: Clinical signs of respiratory distress (e.g., breathing noises, labored breathing, tachypnea, and cyanosis) were observed in males at 400 mg/kg/day and in females at \ge 100 mg/kg/day from Week 33 onwards. Dose-related decrease in body weights was observed in males (-4%, -7%,
L933: and -17% final body weights at 30, 100, and 400 mg/kg/day, respectively) and in females (-8% and -19% final body weights at 30 and 100 mg/kg/day, respectively) mice.
L934: In histological evaluation, treatment-related proliferation of the nasal mucosa and submucosal glands was observed with associated increased incidence of inflammatory infiltration in the submucosa / submucosal glands. Hyaline inclusions and increased secretion were observed at doses \ge 100 mg/kg/day, which could be the reason for the respiratory distress noticed in this study. The incidence of hepatocellular hypertrophy was increased in males at all dose levels. Mean severity and incidence of endometrial cysts in the uterus was increased in females at 100 mg/kg/day, which was associated with increase in uterus organ weight.
L935: In this study macitentan (ACT-064992) did not increase the incidence of neoplastic lesions when tested up to 400 mg/kg/day in male mice and 100 mg/kg/day in female mice, which is a maximum tolerated dose (MTD) in males and females, respectively. The aprocitentan exposure (AUC) at macitentan dose of 100 mg/kg/day in Week 26 provided approximately 34-fold (females) and 50-fold (males) human exposure at the MRHD of aprocitentan (12.5 mg).
L936: ## Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee Conclusions
L937: - The Committee found that the study was acceptable, noting prior Executive CAC concurrence with the protocol.
- The Committee concurred that there were no drug-related neoplasms.
L938: ## **13.1.3.3.2. T-09.159: ACT-064992: 104-Week
## Oncogenicity (Gavage) Study in the Wistar Rat**
L939: Male and female rats (Wistar, 51 rats/sex/group) were treated with macitentan (vehicle; 0.5% methylcellulose in purified water, 10, 50 and 250 mg/kg/day, oral) for a duration of 104 weeks, except for female rats of the 50 and 250 mg/kg dose group. In the female rats, due to clinical signs at doses \ge 50 mg/kg/day and mortality at 250 mg/kg/day, the mid and high dose groups were suspended in weeks 50 and 51 and resumed in week 52 at doses 25 and 50 mg/kg/day.
L940: Body weight was decreased in males at 250 mg/kg/day and females at 50/25 and 250/50 mg/kg/day. Respiratory distress (breathing noises, labored breathing, tachypnea, and cyanosis) was observed in a dose-dependent manner at all doses tested with higher incidences in the females.
L941: In this study, macitentan (ACT-064992) treatment did not increase the incidence of neoplastic lesions when tested up to an MTD. The aprocitentan exposure (AUC) at macitentan dose of 50 mg/kg/day dose in Week 26, provided approximately 8-fold (male) and 14-fold (females) the human AUC at the MRHD for aprocitentan.
L942: ## Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee Conclusions
L943: - The Committee found that the study was acceptable, noting prior Executive CAC concurrence with the protocol.
- The Committee concurred that there were no drug-related neoplasms.
L944: ## 13.1.3.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicity
L945: The Applicant submitted a fertility and early embryonic development study (Segment I) with aprocitentan alone and referred to NDA 204410 (Opsumit) for embryo-fetal development (EFD) studies (Segment II) and pre- and postnatal developmental (PPND) study with macitentan. This was acceptable because aprocitentan represented \ge 50\% of the combined exposure to macitentan and aprocitentan in rats and rabbits, and the plan was agreed upon with the FDA prior to NDA submission. The referenced EFD and PPND studies were reviewed previously under NDA 204410.
L946: During the NDA review cycle, the Applicant submitted additional EFD studies with aprocitentan alone to support their proposed (b) (4) REMS. While the Agency was not obligated to accept those data for review during the NDA review cycle, the data were nonetheless reviewed. The adequacy of the submitted information to address Agency concerns for embryo-fetal risk is discussed in Section 13.2.1. The general conclusion was that the data
L947: compared to the other members of the endothelin receptor antagonist (ERA) class, all of which demonstrate teratogenicity at clinically relevant exposures in rats due to a shared mechanism of action.
L948: ## 13.1.3.4.1. Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L949: **Study Number/Title: T-16-001/ACT-132577E – Fertility Toxicity Study by the Oral Route (Gavage) in the Male Rat (Segment I)**
L950: ## Key Study Findings
L951: - No aprocitentan-related effects on male gonadal function, mating behavior or virility were observed based on reproductive indices and sperm parameters.
- Minimal dilation of the seminiferous tubules in the testes was observed in 1/22 rats at dose of 50 mg/kg/day and 4/22 rats at dose of 250 mg/kg/day.
- Dose-dependent enlarged liver and increased liver weight with associated centrilobular hepatocellular hypertrophy was observed with increasing severity. Hepatocellular vacuolar degeneration/necrosis (minimal to slight) of hypertrophic hepatocytes was observed at 250 mg/kg/day. A resultant dose-dependent minimal and/or slight follicular cell hypertrophy in the thyroid glands was observed, which represents a rat-specific response to increased turnover of thyroid hormone in the liver.
- The no observed effect level for male fertility was 250 mg/kg/day, based on lack of effect on gonadal function, mating behavior and reproductive performance. The NOAEL for general toxicity was determined at 50 mg/kg/day, based on hepatocellular vacuolar degeneration/necrosis at 250 mg/kg/day, which was associated with AUC0-24h values of 468 µg·hr/mL in male rats, approximately 13-fold the AUC at the MRHD.
L952: GLP Compliance: Yes

L972: ## 13.1.3.4.2. Embryo-Fetal Development
L973: EFD studies were cross-referenced from the NDA 204410 for Opsumit (macitentan) and the results are summarized below. Aprocitentan is an active and the major metabolite of macitentan. Its exposure in rats and rabbits represented \ge 50\% of total exposure with administration of macitentan.
L974: ## Rat Embryofetal Development Toxicity Study
L975: A preliminary embryo-fetal toxicity study (Study # T-05.008) was conducted in Wistar rats for the purpose of dose selection in the main embryo-fetal toxicity studies. Administration of macitentan (0, 150, 450 and 1500 mg/kg/day) from Day 6 to Day 17 of the gestation period resulted maternal toxicity at 1500 mg/kg/day and embryo-fetal toxicity at all doses tested. Therefore, 150 mg/kg/day was selected as the highest dose in the main study. Below are the definitive study results.
L976: ## **Study Number/Title: T-12.804/ACT-132577E: ACT-064992: Oral (Gavage) Embryo-Fetal Development Study in the Rat With Toxicokinetic Sampling**
L977: ## Key Study Findings
L978: - Fetal abnormalities, defects in the head/neck, heart vessels and skeletal (skull), were observed at all the doses tested.
- Macitentan treatment was teratogenic in rats starting at the lowest dose tested (3 mg/kg/day), which corresponds to an AUC0-24 h of 36.8 \mu\text{g}\cdot\text{hr}/\text{mL} of aprocitentan. This is approximately equivalent to the exposure at MRHD for aprocitentan based on AUC.

L1011: ## 13.1.3.4.3. Rat Pre- and Postnatal Development Study
L1012: ## **Study Number/Title: T-09.617/ACT-064992: Study for Effects on Pre- and Postnatal Development Including Maternal Function in the Wistar Rat**
L1013: The PPND study was cross-referenced from the macitentan application, NDA 204410, Opsumit, and the results are summarized below.
L1014: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1015: ## F0 Dams
L1016: - Postnatal loss was increased at all dose levels resulting in decreased birth and viability indices.
- Preweaning loss was increased with corresponding decrease in weaning index at 250 mg/kg/day.
- Several pups were found dead during the first litter check after weaning and “no milk in the stomach” was observed in several pups during the lactation period.
L1017: ## F1 Pups
L1018: - Mean bodyweight and body weight gain were reduced on Day 21 and remained reduced on Day 42.
- Balano-preputial separation and opening of vagina were delayed at 250 mg/kg/day.
- At necropsy the following effects were observed.Liver-enlarged hepatocytes at periportal region, bile duct hyperplasia and inflammatory foci in the liver.
- Male reproductive organs – reduced size of testes and epididymides with correlated reduced organ weights in some F1 pups at all treated dose levels; increased incidence of minimal/slight tubular atrophy in F1 males at dam dose of 250 mg/kg/day.
L1019: ## F1 Generation
L1020: - Number of female animals that did not mate or were not pregnant increased.
- Preimplantation loss increased with reduced number of implantation sites and reduced mean number of liver embryos at termination.
- Liver size was reduced with thickened or thickened/enlarged lobes. Hepatocytes were increased in size at periportal location, bile duct hyperplasia and some inflammatory foci were observed in some animals of all treated groups.
- Testes, epididymides and seminal vesicles reduced in size with correlated reduction in organ weights. Increased incidence of minimal/slight tubular atrophy was noticed at 250 mg/kg/day.
L1021: ## NDA 217686
## TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
L1022: No NOAEL was determined for reproduction parameters for F0 dams or F1-pups, based on adverse findings at all doses tested.
L1023: Macitentan treatment exhibited adverse effects in rats starting at the lowest dose of 10 mg/kg/day, which corresponds to apocitentan AUC0-24 h of 69.8 µg*hr/mL, approximately 2-fold the exposure at the MRHD of apocitentan.
L1024: GLP Compliance: Yes

L1035: ## 13.1.4. Other Toxicology / Specialized Studies
L1036: Phototoxicity: No phototoxicity studies was conducted with apocitentan alone but an in vivo study conducted for macitentan (Study No: T-09.061) was referenced, which was deemed to be devoid of phototoxic potential, based on the lack of skin response. In this study administration of macitentan (0, 15 and 60 mg/kg, single, p.o.) in female hairless rats (Ico:OFA-hr/hr, 6/group) did not produce any erythema formation at an AUC0-24h of 140 \mug\cdothr/mL of apocitentan at 60 mg/kg. This is approximately 4-fold the exposure at MRHD for apocitentan.
L1037: ## 13.1.5. Impurities / Degradants
L1038: [REDACTED](b) (4) and [REDACTED](b) (4) are degradation impurities present in both the apocitentan drug substance and drug product. These impurities were reported to be formed by [REDACTED](b) (4). The Applicant proposed a specification limit of not more than (NMT) [REDACTED](b) (4)% and [REDACTED](b) (4)% for [REDACTED](b) (4) in drug substance and drug product, respectively, and
L1039: (b) (4) % for (b) (4) in drug substance, which exceeds the qualification thresholds per the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) Q3A and ICH Q3B.
L1040: ## Mutagenic Potential Evaluation
L1041: (b) (4): The Applicant reported no structural alert for bacterial mutagenicity by conducted in silico analysis (Derek and Leadscope analysis) for (b) (4) and considered it as a Class 5 impurity as per ICH M7. To further support the lack of mutagenic potential for (b) (4) the Applicant conducted a bacterial mutagenicity assay (T-04.072) and it was submitted to NDA 204410. In this assay 5 bacterial strains (TA98, TA100, TA1535, TA1537 and TA102) were used and the (b) (4) was tested up to (b) (4) µg/plate using the plate incorporation method (with or without metabolic activation). This assay was concluded to be negative for mutagenicity for (b) (4).
L1042: (b) (4): No mutagenicity data were provided for the (b) (4). However, this has a similar structure as (b) (4) (Fig 14).
L1043: CDER's Division of Applied Regulatory Science conducted (Q)SAR analysis to assess the potential mutagenicity of (b) (4). The internal (Q)SAR analysis predict no structural alerts for mutagenicity. Therefore, (b) (4) was considered to be nonmutagenic Class 5 impurity as per ICH M7. No additional genotoxicity evaluations were necessary.
L1044: ## General Toxicity
L1045: **Drug substance impurities:** To address the safety of (b) (4) and (b) (4), the Applicant provided a justification supporting reliance on the 39-week dog repeat dose toxicity study. In this study, (b) (4) and (b) (4) were reported to present at (b) (4) % and (b) (4) %, respectively, in the aprocitentan drug substance. According to the study report (Study # T-15.053) the levels reported were (b) (4) mg/kg/day for (b) (4) and (b) (4) mg/kg/day for (b) (4). The NOAEL (5 mg/kg/day) determined for aprocitentan in the 39-week repeat dose study in dogs yields (b) (4) mg/kg/day and (b) (4) mg/kg/day (b) (4). Human equivalent doses are thus (b) (4) mg/day and (b) (4) mg/day (b) (4), based on body surface area calculations assuming a 60 kg adult body weight. These doses are (b) (4) fold and (b) (4) fold the proposed drug substance specification limit of NMT (b) (4) % ( (b) (4) mg) for (b) (4) and NMT (b) (4) % ( (b) (4) mg) for (b) (4). Therefore, the proposed drug substance specifications are supported by the dog data and are considered acceptable.
L1046: **Drug product impurities:** To address the safety of drug product impurities, the Applicant provided a safety justification based on the 26-week rat repeat dose toxicity study. In this study,
L1047: (b) (4) was reported to at (b) (4)% in the aprocitentan. The NOAEL assigned for that study was 50 mg/kg/day, which yielding an (b) (4) dose of (b) (4) mg/kg/day. A human equivalent dose was calculated at (b) (4) mg/day, based on body surface area extrapolation for a 60 kg adult. This dose is ~ (b) (4)-fold (b) (4) the proposed drug substance specification limit of NMT at (b) (4) the proposed drug product specifications are supported by the rat data and are considered acceptable.

L1056: ## 13.2.1. Embryo-Fetal Development
L1057: The initial NDA submission did not contain any EFD studies with aprocitentan alone. Instead, the Applicant relied upon macitentan [Opsumit NDA] EFD data in rats and rabbits, as agreed upon with the Agency. The macitentan EFD studies are discussed in Section 13.1.

L1059: ## Rat Embryofetal Development Toxicity Study
L1060: ## **Study Number/Title: T-22.030/ACT-132577E: ACT-132577E: An Embryo-Fetal Development Study by Oral (Gavage)**
L1061: ## Key Study Findings
L1062: - Dose selection justification was insufficient (i.e., too low) to adequately characterize the potential developmental toxicity of aprocitentan.
- There were no treatment-related adverse effects at doses tested up to the highest dose of 10 mg/kg/day regarding maternal and developmental toxicity, which was associated with an AUC0-24 h of 165000 ng*hr/mL for aprocitentan, approximately 5-fold the AUC at MRHD.
- While the minor observations in fetal visceral and skeletal examinations could not be attributed to aprocitentan treatment given similar observations reported in the control and historical control groups, fetal observations were similar to those reported in studies with the macitentan and other ERAs.

L1073: ## Justification for Dose Selection
L1074: A dose range-finding (DRF) study (Study No. T-22.029) was conducted for aprocitentan (0.3, 1, 3, 10 mg/kg/day) in rats. No mortality was observed in the DRF study. Reduction in food consumption (-21% between Day 6-18 and -15.7% between Day 6-21) was correlated with lower maternal body weight (4.3 to 9.2% at gestational day (GD) 6-9, 10 to 12.1% at GD 9-13, 6.8-9.7% on GD 7-17) at 10 mg/kg/day. Reduction in mean fetal body weights (7%, combined males and females) was observed at 10 mg/kg/day. Other observations including minor clinical signs (suspected dehydration, thin body appearance and hunched position) were not dose limiting. Except a slight reduction of mean fetal body weight (7%) at 10 mg/kg/day, no other treatment related effects were observed in ovarian and uterine examination and litter observations. No treatment related malformations or variations were observed. The Applicant selected 10 mg/kg/day as the highest dose based on a decrease in maternal body weight. Given the critical importance of identifying both a lowest-observed adverse effect level and a NOAEL for embryofetal toxicity with aprocitentan in the rat, doses selection was inadequate.

L1088: ## Justification for Dose Selection
L1089: A DRF study (Study # T-22.027) was conducted for aprocitentan (0.25, 0.75, 2.5 mg/kg/day) in rabbits. The Applicant referred to a nonpregnant rabbit study in which maximum tolerability of aprocitentan was tested. Based on the exposure (AUC_{0-24h} of 3740 \mug*hr/mL) observed at 10 mg/kg/day in this nonpregnant rabbit study, a dose of 2.5 mg/kg/day was selected as the highest dose for the DRF study with an aim to achieve one fourth of the exposure (AUC_{0-24h} of 935 \mug*hr/mL). In the DRF study, no mortality was observed. No treatment-related effects on body weight, food consumption, clinical signs, gravid uterine weights, maternal necropsy, ovarian and uterine parameters and litter parameters, fetal examination parameters were observed at doses tested, and an MTD could not be identified. The high dose tested achieved 29-fold the clinical exposure at the MRHD of 12.5 mg/day, which may be considered appropriate, though a lowest-observed adverse effect level for embryofetal toxicity could not be identified in the rabbit.
L1090: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L1091: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L1092: ## 14.1.1. Plasma Protein Binding
L1093: ## Study B-07.077
L1094: B-07.077 examined the plasma protein binding and plasma/red blood cell partitioning of aprocitentan in vitro using the ^{14}C-labelled derivative ACT-132577B in a concentration range from 0.1 to 300 \mug/mL. The overall mean binding of ACT-132577B to plasma proteins over the whole concentration range in humans was 99%, with a mean free fraction of 1%. There was no obvious concentration-dependent effect in humans. Chromatogram radioactivity associated with ACT-132577B in the plasma and buffer compartments after radio-high pressure liquid chromatography showed that only about 90% of the plasma compartment radioactivity was associated with ACT-132577B, and in the buffer compartment, there were two main peaks that accounted for radioactivity. One peak corresponded to ACT-132577B and the other peak was an unknown chemical. This substance was on the radiochromatogram of ACT-132577 (in the starting material). The Applicant therefore believes that the compound in the dialysate is the result of a selective transfer during the equilibrium dialysis experiment and not a post-experimental degradation product. Therefore, the protein binding values were corrected based on the actual amount of ACT-132577. The corrected plasma protein binding was 99.5%, with a mean corrected free fraction of 0.5%. Plasma/red blood cell partitioning was determined at 500 ng/mL and 100 \mug/mL. The mean association of radioactivity with red blood cells at both concentrations was 4.8%. The blood/plasma ratio was 0.54. This indicates that there was not a significant uptake of radioactivity into red blood cells.
L1095: ## 14.1.2. Metabolism Studies
L1096: ## Study B-14.033
L1097: B-14.033 looked at the metabolic profile of aprocitentan in human liver preparations. Six metabolites were formed along two primary pathways involving hydrolysis to M1 and glucosidation to M3.
L1098: Incubation of radiolabeled aprocitentan, ACT-132577B, with human liver microsomes led to formation of M1 and M2 (4.3% and 2.1% of total chromatogram radioactivity, respectively). M1 was also observed in experiments without microsomes or a nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide phosphate (NADPH)-regenerating system and was present at 9.3% and 6.4% total chromatogram radioactivity, respectively, in comparable conditions.
L1099: Incubation of ACT-132577B with hepatocytes generated metabolites M1-M6. No metabolite contributed to more than 5% total chromatogram radioactivity, and total turnover was 21%. Out of 3 batches, M1-M6 was seen in batch 1, and M1, M2, and M5 were seen in batches 2 or 3. M1 was seen in the control experiment without liver cells and accounted for 8.7% total chromatogram radioactivity after a 24-hour incubation.
L1100: Formation of M1 results from both chemical and enzymatic hydrolysis. M1 is then metabolized to secondary metabolites M2 and M5 through P450/FMO-dependent reactions. M2 undergoes further glucuronidation to M4, and M5 is oxidized to M6 in human hepatocytes.
L1101: ## Study B-14.050
L1102: This study determined the structure of aprocitentan metabolites with liquid chromatography combined with high resolution mass spectrometry. Specifically, structure determination of metabolites was performed from samples from the in vitro metabolite profiling study (B-14.033). Metabolites from rat hepatocytes were also characterized. The proposed metabolic pathways of aprocitentan are presented in Figure 16. In total, it appears that aprocitentan is metabolized along 5 metabolic pathways – hydrolysis to M1, glucosidation to M3, glucuronidation to M8, oxidative debromination then glucuronidation to M10, and N-oxidation at the pyrimidine sulfamide moiety to M9. Metabolite M9 would then subsequently undergo glucuronidation to M22. M1 came from hydrolysis of the sulfamide moiety to an aminopyrimidine and has been shown to be a product of nonenzymatic hydrolysis and a true metabolite (See Study B-05.130 in Section 14.1.4). Metabolites M9 and M26 undergo glucuronidation to metabolites M22 and M10, respectively. Additional downstream metabolites of M1 include M2, M11, M4, M21, M15, M5, M13, M6, and M16.
L1103: Figure 16. Proposed Metabolic Pathways of Aprocitentan
L1104: Source: Applicant-generated figure from Study B-14.050, Figure 3
Abbreviation: GLUC, glucuronic acid
L1105: ## Study B-18.023
L1106: B-18.023 was an in vitro study conducted to identify the human uridine diphosphate-glucuronosyltransferase (UGT) enzymes involved in the formation of the aprocitentan glucoside M3 metabolite. ^{14}\text{C}-labelled aprocitentan was incubated with recombinant human UGT enzymes. UGT enzyme activity is listed in Table 76. Aprocitentan was metabolized to M3 by UGT1A1 and UGT2B7. M3 formation was 1.1% for UGT1A1 and 1.4% for UGT2B7 after 60 mins, indicating turnover was low. Additionally, the chemical hydrolysis product M1 was the most prevalent entity observed in UGT experiments and control incubations without UDPG or UGT enzymes. M1 was similar in all samples ranging from 20 to 23% after 60 mins.

L1110: ## 14.1.3. Transporter Characterization
L1111: ## Study B-17.068
L1112: B-17.068 is an in vitro drug transporter substrate and permeability study to determine if apocitentan is a substrate of P-glycoprotein (P-gp) and breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) and to investigate the in vitro permeability of apocitentan.
L1113: Efflux ratios of apocitentan in MDCKII-MDR1 cells were 2.3 to 3.5 and decreased to 0.8-0.9 in the presence of zosuquidar, a selective P-gp inhibitor. In a few cases, the recoveries were below 70%, but the Applicant considers these samples valid since the Papp values were in agreement with the rest of the values. The data indicates that apocitentan is a substrate of P-gp.
L1114: Efflux ratios of apocitentan in MDCKII-BCRP cells were 7.3 to 23.2 and decreased to 0.8 to 1.3 in the presence of Ko143, a BCRP inhibitor. The data indicates that apocitentan is a substrate of BCRP. In a few cases, the recoveries were below 70%, but the Applicant considers these samples valid since the Papp values were in agreement with the rest of the values. Overall, the data indicates that apocitentan is a substrate of BCRP.
L1115: The apparent absorptive permeability coefficients of aprocitentan in MDCKII cells were 13.6 \times 10^{-6} cm/s, indicating moderate to high permeability.
L1116: ## Study B-10.642
L1117: Study B-10.642 evaluated if aprocitentan is a substrate of human hepatic organic anion transporting polypeptides (OATPs) OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OATP2B1. Uptake of aprocitentan into wild-type and OATP-expressing CHO cells was high and rapid. There was no apparent difference in the uptake of aprocitentan between wild-type and OATP1B1 cells. The uptake of aprocitentan into OATP1B3 and OATP2B1 cells at concentrations less than 4\mu\text{M} was maximally 40% higher than into wild-type cells. No marked difference in the rate of uptake into wild-type and OATP1B1 cells was observed over 0.01 to 300\mu\text{M}. The uptake ratios for OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OATP2B1 were a maximum of 1.1, 1.3, and 1.4, respectively, with incubation times ranging from 0 to 60 seconds, and concentrations ranging from 1\mu\text{M} to 3\mu\text{M}. The positive control of estrone-3-sulphate had an uptake ratio of 4.5, 5.6, and 11.6 for OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OATP2B1, respectively, at 5 minutes at 5\mu\text{M}. The data indicate that cellular uptake of aprocitentan does not depend on human OATP transporters but is driven by passive diffusion, though there may be some contribution from active uptake.
L1118: Additionally, aprocitentan inhibited OATP1B1-mediated uptake of atorvastatin and OATP1B3- and OATP2B1-mediated uptake of estrone-3-sulphate. The mean half maximal inhibitory concentration (\text{IC}_{50}) values for aprocitentan were 21, 56, and 15\mu\text{M} for OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OATP2B1, respectively.
L1119: ## 14.1.4. Drug-Drug Interactions
L1120: ## Study B-05.044
L1121: B-05.044 studied the effect of ACT-064992 (macitentan) and ACT-132577 (aprocitentan) on bile salt transport. Specifically, the potential to inhibit sodium-dependent taurocholate co-transporting polypeptide (NTCP) and human bile salt export pump (BSEP) was investigated. NTCP inhibition was tested in CHO F1p InTM cells over-expressing NTCP. BSEP inhibition was tested in Sf9 membrane vesicles over-expressing BSEP.
L1122: Aprocitentan inhibited NTCP- and BSEP-mediated uptake of taurocholic acid, with a mean \text{IC}_{50} value of 14\mu\text{M} and 50\mu\text{M}, respectively.
L1123: ## Study B-05.130
L1124: B-05.130 studied a panel of cytochrome p450 (CYP) inhibition assays to assess the potential of drug-drug interactions with concomitantly administered CYP450 substrates. Specifically, the potential to inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP2E1, and CYP3A4 was investigated. See Table 77.

L1129: ## **Study B-13.098**
L1130: B-13.098 studied the inhibition potential of apocitentan on P-gp activity in Caco-2 cells using digoxin as a prototypical substrate. Concentrations of apocitentan ranged from 3 to 70\mu\text{M}. Higher concentrations could not be studied due to limited solubility in the transport buffer. The absorptive permeability of digoxin increased in a concentration-dependent manner, as in the presence of 3\mu\text{M} and 70\mu\text{M} of apocitentan, digoxin permeability increased by 1% and 35%, respectively.
L1131: ## **Study B-20.030**
L1132: B-20.030 assessed the inhibition potential of apocitentan on the activity of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 transporters in an assay with a pre-incubation period in CHO cells expressing each transporter. Apocitentan inhibited OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 mediated uptake of atorvastatin acid with \text{IC}_{50} values of 19 and 27\mu\text{M}, respectively. Cyclosporin A was used as a control inhibitor, and inhibited uptake of atorvastatin acid with \text{IC}_{50} values of 0.1 and 0.3\mu\text{M} for OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, respectively. Nonspecific binding was assessed in CHO wild-type cells at concentrations of 19 and 27\mu\text{M}, and the fraction recovered from incubation solutions was 45% and 83%.
L1133: ## **Study B-17.069**
L1134: B-17.069 assessed the potential of apocitentan to alter the enzymatic activity of CYP2C8 and CYP2C19 due to time-dependent inhibition, as well as the inhibition potential of apocitentan on the activity of UGT1A1 and UGT2B7. Experiments used human liver microsomes for CYP2C8, recombinant CYP2C19, and recombinant UGT enzymes for UGT1A1 and UGT2B7. The marker reactions for UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 were estradiol 3-\beta-glucuronidation and 3'-azido-3'-
L1135: deoxythymidine 5-\beta-glucuronidation, respectively. Paclitaxel 6\alpha-hydroxylation was used as a marker reaction for CYP2C8, and formation of 4-hydroxymephenytoin was monitored as a marker for CYP2C19 activity. Gemfibrozil 1-O-\beta glucuronide and ticlopidine were reference inhibitors of CYP2C8 and CYP2C19, respectively. IC50 shift was determined as the ratio of the IC50 values without and with NADPH and indicates the change in enzyme activity during the pre-incubation period. The IC50 shift for apocitentan in CYP2C8 was 1.0 and was not determined in CYP2C19 because the IC50 with pre-incubation with and without NADPH were both &gt;100\muM, whereas the IC50 shift for gemfibrozil 1-O-\beta -glucuronide and ticlopidine for CYP2C8 and CYP2C19 was 24 and 7.8, respectively. This indicates that apocitentan is not a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP2C8 and CYP2C19. Apocitentan was a weak inhibitor of UGT1A1 and UGT2B7, with IC50 values of 23 and 43\muM, respectively. Nonspecific binding of apocitentan in the UGT inhibition assays was 3.9% and 3.8% for UGT1A1 and UGT2B7, respectively.
L1136: ## **Study B-21.017**
L1137: B-21.017 assessed the time-dependent inhibition of CYP1A1 and CYP2B6 by apocitentan in human liver microsomes. Marker reactions for CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 were phenacetin-O-deethylation and bupropion hydroxylation, respectively. Furafylline and ticlopidine were run in parallel as positive controls for CYP1A2 and CYP2B6, respectively. IC50 shift was determined as the ratio of the IC50 values without and with NADHP and indicate the change in enzyme activity during the pre-incubation period. The IC50 shift for apocitentan in CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 was not determined, as values with and without NADPH were &gt;100\muM, whereas the IC50 shift for furafylline and ticlopidine for CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 was 94 and 131, respectively. This indicates that apocitentan is not a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP1A2 and CYP2B6.
L1138: ## **Study B-05.107**
L1139: B-05.107 assessed the potential of apocitentan to induce human CYP3A4, CYP2C9, and CYP1A2. Activation of the human pregnane X receptor (PXR) by apocitentan was tested in a reporter gene assay, and induction of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, and CYP1A2 was tested in three batches of primary human hepatocytes on an mRNA and/or enzyme activity level. Nifedipine, diclofenac, and phenacetin were used as marker substrates for CYP3A4, CYP2C9, and CYP1A2 activity, respectively. Rifampicin was a positive control in the PXR activation assay. Apocitentan was tested for induction at concentration up to 10\muM. The positive control for the CYP3A4 and CYP2C9 regulation assays was rifampicin, and the positive control for the CYP1A2 regulation assay was omeprazole.
L1140: Apocitentan activated PXR with a half maximal effective concentration (EC50) of 7.2 to 8.7\muM, which is less potent than the positive control rifampicin (EC50 0.5 to 0.6\muM). Apocitentan demonstrated generally concentration-dependent increases in CYP3A4 mRNA and enzyme activity. In the first batch of hepatocytes, the fold activity was 1.36, 1.57, 2.90, and 3.87 at 0.1, 1, 3, and 10\muM, respectively. In the second batch of hepatocytes, the fold activity was 0.99, 1.13, 1.42, and 1.36 at 0.1, 1, 3, and 10\muM, respectively. In the third batch of hepatocytes, the fold activity was 1.35, 1.47, 1.51, and 1.28 at 0.1, 1, 3, and 10\muM, respectively. CYP3A4 mRNA levels also increased dose dependently, with 10\muM producing a 6.72-, 2.77-, and 2.84-fold induction in each hepatocyte batch compared to a 9.59-, 5.30-, and 4.89-fold induction in each
L1141: hepatocyte batch for 10\muM rifampicin. There were no dose-dependent increases in CYP2C9 activity; however, it does appear that there was a small dose-dependent increase in CYP2C9 mRNA levels. The 10\muM dose produced a 1.59-, 1.25-, and 1.29-fold induction in each hepatocyte batch compared to a 2.42-, 2.04-, and 2.90-fold induction at 10\muM rifampicin. There were dose-dependent increases in CYP1A2 activity with increasing aprocitentan dose. The 10\muM dose produced a 3.05-, 2.28- and 2.71-fold increase in activity, compared to a 19.73-, 14.23-, and 10.83-fold increase in activity with omeprazole.
L1142: ## **Study B-12.103**
L1143: B-12.103 assessed the potential for aprocitentan to competitively inhibit CYP2C8 and time-dependent inhibition of CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 in vitro using human liver microsomes and cytochrome P450 isoform-specific marker transformations. The reactions used paclitaxel 6\alpha-hydroxylation, diclofenac 4'-hydroxylation, dextromethorphan-O-demethylation, and testosterone 6\beta-hydroxylation as marker reactions for CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4, respectively. Reference inhibitors included quercetin, tienilic acid, paroxetine, and mibefradil for CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 respectively.
L1144: Aprocitentan was an inhibitor of CYP2C8 activity (IC_{50}=23\muM). Aprocitentan had no time-dependent inhibition of CYP2C9, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4 activities, as the shift values were 1.2, 0.9, and not calculable due to absence of inhibition, respectively, compared to reference shift values of 190, 64, and 29, respectively.
L1145: ## **Study B-13.097**
L1146: Study B-13.097 assessed the inhibition potential of aprocitentan on the activity of efflux and uptake organic anion transporters (OATs) OAT1 and OAT3, organic cation transporters (OCTs) OCT1 and OCT2, multidrug and toxin extrusion transporters (MATE)-1 and MATE2K, and BCRP. Probe substrates (\muM) were MPP^{+} (5), pulmonary arterial hypertension (10), furosemide (5), MPP^{+} (5), metformin (50), and 4-(4-dimethylaminostyryl)-N-methylpyridinium (1), and positive control inhibitors (\muM) were repaglinide (600), probenecid (100), probenecid (100), imipramine (300), cimetidine (100), and ondansetron (100) for OCT-1, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1, and MATE2K, respectively. For BCRP assessment in BCRP-MDCK cells, cladribine was used as a probe substrate, and Ko143 was used as a positive control inhibitor.
L1147: Aprocitentan showed inhibitory potential for OAT1 (IC_{50}=4.7\muM). Aprocitentan also showed inhibitory potential of BCRP (IC_{50}=5.7\muM). Aprocitentan had some inhibition in a concentration-dependent manner for OAT3 (IC_{50}=1.5\muM). There was no effect observed on OCT1, OCT2, or MATE2K or MATE1 at the concentrations tested (up to 100\muM).
L1148: ## **14.1.5. In Vitro Summary**
L1149: Plasma protein binding of aprocitentan in humans was 99.5%, and the mean blood/plasma ratio was 0.54, indicating limited binding to red blood cells. Aprocitentan was highly permeable across cell membranes. When studies were conducted to determine if aprocitentan is dependent on hepatic uptake transporters OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, the uptake of aprocitentan in wild-type and OATP-expressing cells was high and rapid without much difference between the two cell
L1150: line types, indicating that hepatic uptake of aprocitentan is mostly due to passive diffusion. Aprocitentan is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP; however, aprocitentan has high permeability across cell membranes, and PK was dose-proportional up to 100 mg, indicating a limited role for efflux transporters in oral absorption. Further, aprocitentan was mostly metabolized prior to excretion. Therefore, impact on aprocitentan exposure due to P-gp or BCRP inhibitors affecting absorption or excretion is not expected. Only 0.2% of the administered dose was excreted unchanged in the urine, so change in uptake and efflux by renal transporter inhibitors or inducers would not likely have clinical relevance.
L1151: Aprocitentan inhibited uptake of several model substrates for several drug transport proteins, with the strongest inhibition seen on OAT1/3 and BCRP (IC_{50} values ranging from 1.5 to 5.7\muM). Inhibition of OATP, NTCP, and bile salt export pump was also observed, but to a lesser extent (IC_{50} values ranging from 14 to 50\muM). IC_{50} values for P-gp, OCT_{1/2}, or MATE1/2K were around or above the highest tested concentration. It was determined in vitro that aprocitentan does not inhibit P-gp because the intestinal luminal concentration of the interacting drug/IC_{50} is 1.83 for a 25 mg dose of aprocitentan, which is less than the threshold of 10. BCRP showed potential to inhibit BCRP in vitro because the intestinal luminal concentration of the interacting drug/IC_{50} value was 32 for a 25 mg dose of aprocitentan, which is greater than the threshold of 10. A clinical drug-drug interaction study for BCRP was conducted (see Section 14.2). R values for OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 were 1.007 and 1.003, respectively, which is below the cutoff of 1.1, indicating that aprocitentan does not have the potential to inhibit OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. OAT1 and OAT3 I_{max,u}/IC_{50} values were 0.026 and 0.083, respectively. As these values are lower than the cutoff of 0.1, aprocitentan does not inhibit OAT. As the IC_{50} for OCT and MATE were &gt;100\muM, aprocitentan does not inhibit these transporters.
L1152: Metabolite M1 is formed by hydrolysis and was formed with and without the presence of enzymes. In human hepatocytes, 6 metabolites were found across all batches (M1-M6). M1 was present in the highest concentrations, followed by secondary and tertiary metabolites M2, M5, M4, and M6. The M3 glucoside metabolite was seen in a single hepatocyte batch at lower levels than the other observed metabolites. UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 were shown to catalyze the formation of M3.
L1153: Aprocitentan had no or minimal inhibition on most human CYP enzymes, including CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2D6, and CYP2E1. All members of the CYP2C family were inhibited, with IC_{50} values ranging from 15 to 31\muM. A K_i value of 11\muM was determined on CYP2C9. Aprocitentan also inhibited CYP3A4 in vitro for both the midazolam and testosterone markers, with IC_{50} values of 7.3\muM and 11\muM, respectively. Aprocitentan did not inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4 in a time-dependent manner. Aprocitentan inhibited UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 (IC_{50} values of 23\muM and 43\muM, respectively). For CYP3A4, assuming a conservative unbound fraction in plasma of 1% and a correction of K_i for the fraction of unbound drug in the test system by 0.8 (in vitro tests indicated values between 0.8 and 1.0), the R_1 and R_{1,gut} values for CYP3A4 were 1.01 and 37, respectively. The R_1 value was below the recommended threshold of 1.02, but the R_{1,gut} value was above the threshold of 11. A clinical drug-drug interaction study for CYP3A4 was conducted (Section 14.2).
L1154: Aprocitentan activated human PXR with an EC_{50} of 7.2 to 8.7\muM. Concentration-dependent increases in CYP3A4 mRNA and enzyme activity were observed. CYP3A4 activity was
L1155: increased by up to 3.9-fold, and CYP3A4 mRNA was increased by up to 6.7-fold at the highest aprocitentan concentration (10\muM), though no relevant changes in CYP1A2 and CYP2C9 activity were observed. Of note, the expected aprocitentan C_{\text{max},u} at steady state is about 0.07\muM.
L1156: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1157: ## Study AC-080-101: Single-Center, Three-Part Phase 1 Study to Investigate the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamics of ACT-132577 in Healthy Male and Female Subjects
L1158: ## Study Design
L1159: This three-part Phase 1 study was composed of Part A, an open-label, single-dose, Phase 1 pilot study; Part B, a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled parallel-group, single ascending dose Phase 1 study (including food effect); and Part C, a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-group, multiple ascending dose Phase 1 study (including healthy elderly subjects). This study used aprocitentan in capsules.
L1160: In Part A, PK, safety, and tolerability was assessed for a single 5 mg dose. In Part B, PK was assessed for single doses of 25, 100, 300, or 600 mg. Food effect was also assessed. In Part C, multiple doses of aprocitentan in healthy male and female subjects were assessed. Doses studied included 5, 25, or 100 mg administered for 10 days. PK in elderly subjects were also assessed.
L1161: ## Results
L1162: After a single dose, aprocitentan was more rapidly absorbed at lower doses than higher doses, with a median T_{\text{max}} of 3 hours after a single 5 mg dose compared to 9 hours after a single 600 mg dose (Table 78). After a single dose, C_{\text{max}} and AUC_{0-\infty} were less than dose proportional (Table 78).

L1195: After multiple-dose administration, mean increases from baseline in body weight were observed in aprocitentan groups, especially in the 100 mg dose group and the elderly group, where the mean change in body weight ranged from -0.5 to +2.5 kg during the study. The placebo group mean change from baseline ranged from -1.1 to +0.9 kg. Following multiple doses of 5, 25, or 100 mg aprocitentan, there was a mean decrease in hemoglobin from baseline of 0.53 to 1.54 g/dL. Mean decreases from baseline in those receiving placebo ranged from 0.22 to 1.22 g/dL. The decrease appears to be dose-related, with median decreases from baseline of 1, 1.3, and 1.6 g/dL for 5, 25, and 100 mg doses, respectively. The median decrease in hemoglobin for those on placebo was 1.1. There were also decreases in hematocrit and red blood cells.
L1196: ## Agency Assessment
L1197: *Aprocitentan exposure increased with food and was higher in females and the elderly. Aprocitentan is intended for chronic use and these differences in AUC are relatively minor; therefore, no dosage adjustments are warranted for food, sex, or age. At steady state, there were treatment- and dose-related decreases in hemoglobin and hematocrit, and after multiple doses of 100 mg aprocitentan, body weight increased more compared to subjects receiving placebo.*
L1198: ## **Study AC-080-104: Single-Center, Open-Label Study With 14C-Radiolabeled ACT-132577 to Investigate the Mass Balance, Pharmacokinetics, and Metabolism Following Single Oral Administration to Healthy Male Subjects**
L1199: ## Study Design
L1200: This study was a single-center, open-label mass balance study with 14C-radiolabeled aprocitentan. A single oral dose of aprocitentan was administered in a capsule, followed by an observation period of 14 days where blood, urine, and feces samples were collected.
L1201: ## Results
L1202: The mean age of the 6 enrolled subjects was 63.0 years (range 61-64 years) with a mean body mass index of 25.23 kg/m2 (range 22.2-27.2 kg/m2). After administration, 52.12% of the administered dose was recovered in the urine, and 24.79% of the administered dose was recovered in the feces. Total cumulative recovery ranged from 65.8% to 82.9%, with 5 out of 6 subjects having a total cumulative recovery of \ge75%.
L1203: Exposure to 14C-radioactivity was lower in whole blood compared to plasma, and the estimated blood/plasma ratio based on Cmax and AUC0-\infty was 0.59 and 0.63, respectively. The mean Cmax (966 ng/mL) and median Tmax (5.0 h) of apocitentan in plasma were comparable to that of 14C-radioactivity (mean Cmax 1060 ng eq/mL; median Tmax 4.0 h) (Table 86 and Table 87).

L1210: In plasma, aprocitentan was mostly detected unchanged (94.3% of the total radioactivity of the sample). The only detected metabolite with a relative abundance of more than 1% of the total radioactivity was metabolite M29 (4.5% of the total radioactivity of the sample).
L1211: In urine, the main products were M3 (46.4% total radioactivity), M6 (8.5% total radioactivity), M16 (8.2% total radioactivity), and A1 (9.7% total radioactivity). In feces, the main products were M1 (41.5% total radioactivity), and unchanged aprocitentan (27.4% total radioactivity) (Table 88).
L1212: There were two metabolic pathways that contributed to 25% or more of the 14C-radioactive elimination: glucosidation to M3 accounting for 25.2% of the administered dose (24.3% urine, 0.8% feces), and hydrolysis of the sulfamide moiety to the corresponding aminopyrimidine of aprocitentan to M1 (10.3% feces, 1.7% urine). After formation of M1, several reactions occur, such as cleavage to M13 (0.8% urine) with further oxidation to A1 (5.1% urine, 1.3% feces), cleavage to M5 (0.7% urine) followed by oxidation of M6 (4.5% urine) and M16 (4.3% urine, 0.4% feces). Other metabolites formed from M1 include A4 (0.2% feces), A5 (0.8% feces), M4
L1213: (0.9% urine), and M11 (0.4% feces). Overall, elimination through M1 and its metabolites accounted for 31.5% of the administered dose.

L1218: ## Agency Assessment
L1219: The C_{max} and T_{max} of aprocitentan in plasma were comparable with the concentration-time profile of ^{14}C-radioactivity; though the mean AUC_{0-\infty} for aprocitentan in plasma was smaller than the AUC_{0-\infty} for total ^{14}C-radioactivity in plasma (see Table 86 and Table 87). The primary elimination route for aprocitentan and its metabolites is through the urine (52% of the drug material), and 25% was eliminated fecally. In plasma, unchanged aprocitentan accounted for most of the radioactivity. In urine, the main products were M3, M6, M16, and A1. In feces, the main products were unchanged aprocitentan and M1. Two significant metabolic pathways are glucosidation of aprocitentan to M3 and hydrolysis to M1 and subsequent metabolites. For a discussion on the enzymes responsible for glucosidation and hydrolysis, please see Section 14.1.2.
L1220: ## **Study AC-080-105: A Single-Center, Open-Label, Single-Dose Study to Investigate the Effect of Severe Renal Impairment on the Pharmacokinetics of ACT-132577**
L1221: ## **Design**
L1222: The primary objective was to investigate the PK of aprocitentan in those with severe renal impairment compared to healthy subjects. Aprocitentan 50 mg was administered in a capsule, and blood samples were obtained over 24 hours and in the mornings of Days 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, and 12 for PK analysis. 16 subjects were enrolled in two groups: Group A (8 healthy subjects), and Group B (8 subjects with severe renal function impairment). Healthy subjects were matched to renal impairment subjects based on age, sex, body weight, and height.
L1223: ## **Results**
L1224: PK was similar after a single oral dose of 50 mg in healthy subjects and subjects with severe renal impairment (Table 89). C_{\text{max}} was similar between groups, with a geometric mean ratio of 1.04 (90% confidence interval [CI] of 0.85 to 1.28) (Table 90). The T_{\text{max}} was 5 hours and 7.6 hours in healthy subjects and those with severe renal impairment, respectively. Clearance was lower in those with severe renal impairment and half-life was longer at 53.2 hours in those with severe renal impairment compared to 47.4 hours in healthy subjects. AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-\infty} were 32% and 34% higher in those with severe renal impairment, respectively (Table 90). Plasma protein binding was similar between healthy subjects and those with severe renal impairment, ranging from 99.04% to 99.19%.

L1235: ## Agency Assessment
L1236: Generally, the PK of a single 50 mg dose of aprocitentan was similar between normal subjects and those with severe renal impairment. The geometric mean ratio for C_{max} was 1.04, meaning there was no clinically relevant difference between the C_{max} in those with severe renal function and healthy subjects. The AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-\infty} were 32% and 34% higher, respectively, in those with severe renal impairment compared to those with normal renal function. Based on the results of this study, aprocitentan can be administered to those with severe renal impairment without the need for dose adjustment. This also means that those with moderate and mild renal impairment may take aprocitentan without dose adjustment.
L1237: *The Applicant defined subjects with severe renal impairment as those with an eGFR between 15 mL/min/1.73 m2 and 29 mL/min/1.73 m2 (inclusive) and subjects with normal renal function \ge 90 mL/min/1.73 m2. Upon review, when eGFR was converted for each subject to mL/min, all subjects in the normal renal function group maintained an eGFR \ge 90 mL/min; however, one subject in the severe renal impairment group had an eGFR &gt; 30 mL/min (35 mL/min). This minor deviation is not considered significant in the interpretation of the study results.*
L1238: ## **Study ID-080-109: An Open-Label Phase 1 Study to Investigate the Effect of Moderate Hepatic Impairment Due to Liver Cirrhosis on the Pharmacokinetics of a Single Dose of 25 mg Aprocitentan**
L1239: ## **Design**
L1240: The primary objective was to investigate the PK of a single dose of 25 mg aprocitentan in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment due to liver cirrhosis compared to healthy subjects. Aprocitentan was administered as a tablet, and blood samples were drawn through 312 hours postdose. 16 subjects were enrolled in two groups: Group 1 (8 subjects with moderate hepatic impairment, Child-Pugh Grade B), and Group 2 (8 healthy subjects). Healthy subjects were matched to subjects with moderate hepatic impairment with regard to sex, age, body weight, and height.
L1241: ## **Results**
L1242: The PK of aprocitentan after a single oral dose of 25 mg was similar between those with moderate hepatic impairment and healthy subjects (Table 91). Peak plasma concentrations were reached in 4 hours in both groups. The geometric mean ratio (90% CI) for Cmax was 1.03 (0.86-1.24) (Table 92). Clearance was lower in those with moderate hepatic impairment, and half-life was longer (Table 91). The AUC0-t and AUC0-\infty were 22% and 23% higher, respectively, in those with moderate hepatic impairment compared to healthy subjects (Table 92). There were no differences in plasma protein binding between those with moderate hepatic impairment and healthy subjects, with mean values ranging from 98.7% to 99% (Table 91).

L1253: ## **Study ID-080-107: A Single-Center, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Randomized Study to Investigate the Tolerability, Safety, and Pharmacokinetics After Multiple-Dose Administration of 25 mg Aprocitentan in Healthy Japanese and Caucasian Subjects**
L1254: ## **Design**
L1255: This was a single-center, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized Phase 1 study in healthy Japanese and Caucasian subjects. Caucasian subjects were matched to Japanese for sex and body weight. 25 mg aprocitentan was administered in tablets orally for 10 days in the fasted condition. A total of 10 Japanese and 10 Caucasian healthy male and female subjects (1:1 sex ratio) between 20 and 45 years were included in the study. The primary objective of this study was to evaluate the relative PK characteristics of multiple-dose administration of 25 mg aprocitentan in Japanese versus Caucasian healthy subjects. The PK analysis set included 4 male and 4 female subjects that received active treatment (1 male and 1 female received placebo).
L1256: ## **Results**
L1257: The PK of aprocitentan after administration of 25 mg once daily for 10 days was similar in Japanese and Caucasian subjects. Steady state was reached between Day 7 and Day 10, with a geometric mean peak-trough fluctuation of 43.91 and 52.73% for Japanese and Caucasian subjects, respectively (Table 93). Accumulation at steady state was similar for both groups, with geometric mean accumulation index values of 2.93 and 3.05 for Japanese and Caucasian subjects, respectively (Table 93). The C_{max} geometric means ratio after the last dose was 0.94 for males, 1.1 for females, and 1.0 overall (Table 94). The AUC_{0-\infty} after the last dosing interval was 0.99 for males, 1.2 for females, and 1.1 overall (Table 94).

L1268: ## **Study ID-080-103: A Single-Center, Open-Label, Two-Treatment Study to Investigate the Effect of ACT-132577 at Steady State on the Pharmacokinetics of Midazolam and its Metabolite 1-Hydroxy Midazolam in Healthy Male Subjects**
L1269: ## **Design**
L1270: This study was a single-center, open-label, single-sequence, two-treatment Phase 1 study. The primary objective was to evaluate the effect of aprocitentan on the PK of midazolam and 1-hydroxy midazolam. All subjects received a single dose of 8 mg midazolam on Day 1 in the fasted condition, and a 24-hour PK profile was collected (Treatment A). Then, on Day 2, all subjects received a single dose of 150 mg aprocitentan. On Days 3, 4, and 5, a trough (predose) blood sample was collected, then 50 mg aprocitentan was administered (Treatment B1). On Day 6, a trough (predose) blood sample was collected, and a single dose of 8 mg midazolam was administered plus a single dose of 50 mg aprocitentan, and a 24-hour PK profile was collected (Treatment B2). Aprocitentan was administered in capsules.
L1271: ## **Results**
L1272: Steady state aprocitentan plasma concentrations had minimal effect on the PK of midazolam (Table 95). The geometric mean ratios (90% Cis) for AUC_{0-t}, AUC_{0-\infty}, and C_{max} were 1.14 (1.06-1.23), 1.14 (1.06-1.22), and 1.04 (0.88-1.23), respectively (Table 96). Steady-state aprocitentan plasma concentrations also had minimal effect on 1-hydroxy midazolam PK (Table 95). The geometric mean ratios (90% Cis) for AUC_{0-t}, AUC_{0-\infty}, and C_{max} were 0.95 (0.89-1.03), 0.98 (0.91-1.05), and 0.86 (0.70-1.05) (Table 96). Additionally, half-life for 1-hydroxy midazolam significantly increased from 5.8 to 7.7 hours (p=0.0141) (Table 95, Table 96).
