L1279: ## Agency Assessment
L1280: *Steady state aprocitentan plasma concentrations did not have a clinically meaningful effect on midazolam PK, as all 90% CIs for the geometric mean ratios for AUC0-t, AUC0-∞, and Cmax were within the 0.8 to 1.25 interval. Steady state aprocitentan plasma concentrations did not have any effect on AUC0-t or AUC0-∞ of 1-hydroxy midazolam. However, the Cmax of 1-hydroxy midazolam was reduced, and the half-life increased by about 2 hours. Overall, the results of this study indicate that aprocitentan does not alter the PK of midazolam or its metabolite to a clinically relevant extent; therefore, aprocitentan can be administered with CYP3A4 substrates without dose adjustment.*
L1281: ## **Study AC-080-106: A Single-Center, Open-Label, One-Sequence, Two-Treatment Study to Investigate the Effect of ACT-132577 at Steady State on the Pharmacokinetics of Rosuvastatin in Healthy Male Subjects**
L1282: ## **Design**
L1283: This is a prospective, single-center, open-label, one-sequence, two-treatment Phase 1 study. The primary objective was to evaluate the effect of aprocitentan at steady state on the PK of a single dose of rosuvastatin in healthy male subjects. Each subject received the following treatments: Treatment A – in the morning of Day 1, a single dose of 10 mg rosuvastatin was administered in the fasted state followed by an observation period of 96 hours; Treatment B1 – doses of 25 mg aprocitentan were administered once daily from Day 5 to Day 12; and Treatment B2 – in the morning of Day 13, a single dose of 10 mg rosuvastatin was administered in the fasted state concomitantly with 25 mg aprocitentan, followed by an observation period of 120 hours. Doses of 25 mg aprocitentan were administered once daily from Day 14 to Day 17. Aprocitentan was administered as a capsule.
L1284: ## **Results**
L1285: Administration with aprocitentan increased the C_{max} of rosuvastatin by 40% (90% CI of the geometric means: 1.19-1.65) (Table 97, Table 98). The half-life decreased by 21% (90% CI of the geometric means: 0.6-1.03) (Table 97, Table 98). AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-\infty} were not affected with geometric mean ratios of 1.07 and 0.99, respectively (Table 97, Table 98). The 90% CIs for AUC were contained within the 0.80 to 1.25 reference interval (Table 98). Mean half-life of rosuvastatin decreased from 17.9 hours to 14.1 hours, though this change was not significant (p=0.14) (Table 97, Table 98).

L1301: ## **Study ID-080-110: A Single-Center, Open-Label, Randomized, Two-Way Crossover Phase 1 Study to Compare the Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics of Different Tablet Formulations of Aprocitentan in Healthy Subjects**
L1302: ## Design
L1303: This was a prospective, open-label, randomized, two-way crossover Phase 1 study to determine the bioequivalence of aprocitentan formulations A and B. Formulation A was the reference product and had [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1304: Formulation B was the test product and had [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1305: ## Results
L1306: The PK of aprocitentan between the two formulations was similar. Both formulations had a T_{max} of 4 hours (Table 99). The half-life, apparent clearance, and apparent volume of distribution were also similar between both Formulation A and Formulation B (Table 99). Formulation B was bioequivalent to Formulation A, as the geometric mean ratios (90% CIs) for C_{max}, AUC_{0-t}, and AUC_{0-\infty} were 0.97 (0.94-1.0), 0.97 (0.95-0.99), and 0.97 (0.94-0.99), respectively (Table 100).

L1320: ## **Study ID-080-108: Prospective, Single-Center, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo- and Moxifloxacin-Controlled, 4-Way Crossover Phase 1 Study to Assess the Effect of Multiple Therapeutic and Supratherapeutic Doses of Aprocitentan on the QT Interval Duration in Healthy Subjects**
L1321: ## **Design**
L1322: This study was a prospective, single-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and moxifloxacin-controlled (open-label), 2-way crossover Phase 1 study. The primary objective was to demonstrate, using concentration-QT interval (QT) analysis in healthy male and female subjects, that multiple-dose administration of oral therapeutic and supratherapeutic doses of aprocitentan do not have a clinically significant effect on the QT interval. Aprocitentan was administered as 25 mg tablets. There were 4 periods in which subjects were to receive, in a randomly assigned sequence, each of the following 4 treatments: Treatment A – repeated administration of a 25 mg oral dose of aprocitentan from Day 1 to Day 10, followed by 18 days of observation; Treatment B – repeated administration of a 100 mg oral dose or aprocitentan from Day 1 to Day 10, followed by 18 days of observation; Treatment C – repeated administration of an aprocitentan-matching placebo once daily from Day 1 to Day 10, followed by 18 days of observation; and Treatment D – repeated administration of an oral dose of aprocitentan-matching placebo once daily from Day 1 to Day 9, and on Day 10, oral administration of one single 400 mg moxifloxacin tablet open-label, followed by 18 days of observation. Each of the 4 periods were separated by a minimum of 2 days.
L1323: ## **Results**
L1324: The QT results are discussed in Section 7.6.9.2, and the PK results for aprocitentan are discussed here. C_{max} and AUC_{\tau} increased approximately dose-proportionally, and the accumulation index was about 3 (Table 101).

L1328: ## Agency Assessment
L1329: *The PK of aprocitentan in Study ID-080-108 was similar to the PK in other Phase 1 studies conducted in healthy volunteers.*
L1330: ## **Study AC-080-102: A Single-Center, Double-Blind, Randomized, Placebo-Controlled, Two-Way Crossover Study to Evaluate Potential Body Weight Increase Induced by Fluid Retention After Administration of ACT-132577 in Healthy Male Subjects on a High-Sodium Diet**
L1331: ## **Design**
L1332: This was a prospective, single-center, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, two-way crossover, Phase 1 study in healthy male subjects on a high sodium diet (HSD). The primary objective was to evaluate the effect of aprocitentan on body weight in healthy male subjects on an HSD. Aprocitentan was administered in capsules.
L1333: Subjects were randomized to one of three aprocitentan dose levels and placebo and to one of two treatment sequences (A-B or B-A) in a 1:1 ratio. Treatment A received 10, 25, or 50 mg of aprocitentan once daily from Day 1 to Day 9. Subjects followed an HSD from Day -3 to Day 9. After the last dose of aprocitentan, subjects stopped the HSD and were observed for 10–12 days. Treatment B received placebo once daily from Day 1 to Day 9. Subjects were on an HSD for Day -3 to Day 9. After the last intake of placebo, subjects stopped the HSD and were observed for 10–12 days. The two treatment periods were separated by a washout of at least 7 days.
L1334: ## **Results**
L1335: After multiple dosing for 9 days, C_{\text{trough}} increased roughly dose proportionally (mean trough concentrations of 781, 1817, and 3694 ng/mL for the 10 mg, 25 mg, and 50 mg doses, respectively).
L1336: The 10 mg aprocitentan dose was considered noninferior to placebo because the upper limit of the 90% CI did not exceed 1 kg (mean increase of +0.43 kg, CI 0.05–0.80). The 25 mg and 50 mg doses had an upper limit of the 90% CI that exceeded 1 kg, so noninferiority was not concluded (mean body weight increases of +0.77 kg, CI 0.03–1.51 and +0.83 kg, CI 0.33–1.32, respectively).
L1337: A decrease in Hb was seen in those on aprocitentan compared to placebo. Placebo-corrected differences between baseline and Day 9 were -3.1, -3.3, and -5.5 g/L for the 10 mg, 25 mg, and 50 mg doses of aprocitentan, respectively. There were slight, nondose-dependent decreases in Hct seen, as placebo-corrected differences between baseline and Day 9 were -0.5, -1, and 0 g/L for 10, 25, and 50 mg, respectively.
L1338: Aprocitentan did not affect sodium excretion parameters in a clear way, as sodium clearance, \text{FE}_{\text{Na}}, and \text{FE}_{\text{Li}} (a marker of proximal tubule sodium reabsorption) were unchanged during the 10 hour collection period on Days 1 and 9. Compared with placebo, there were small differences in sodium excretion rates during the 10 hour collection periods on Days 1 and 9. On Day 9 but not on Day 1, there was a lower sodium excretion rate with 10 mg aprocitentan than with placebo; however, this was not confirmed at the 25 mg dose level. Further, on Day 1 and Day 9, there was a consistently higher sodium excretion rate with 50 mg aprocitentan than placebo (see Figure 17).
L1339: Figure 17. Urinary Sodium Excretion Rates on Day 1 and Day 9 (Mean\pmSD)
L1340: Source: Applicant-generated figure from AC-080-102 Study Report, Doc No D-18.046, Figure 11-9
Abbreviations: h, hour; Na, sodium; SD, standard deviation
L1341: There were no clear patterns of changes in body fluid-regulating hormones between baseline and Day 9.
L1342: There appeared to be a small, nondose-dependent increase in extracellular water (ECW) with all three doses of aprocitentan, and there did not appear to be an increase in intracellular water.
L1343: There was no effect on systolic blood pressure (SBP), but there was a small decrease in diastolic blood pressure (DBP) between baseline and Day 9 (placebo-corrected decrease of -3.6, -4.6, and -4.8 mmHg for the 10, 25, and 50 mg aprocitentan doses, respectively) (Table 102).

L1349: ## Agency Assessment
L1350: *The 10 mg dose is considered noninferior to placebo for body weight gain; however, noninferiority cannot be confirmed for the 25 and 50 mg doses, as the 90% CI upper limit exceeded 1 kg. While it appears that there may be some hemodilution from aprocitentan treatment while on an HSD, there was no clear dose-response relationship. There was also a nondose-dependent increase in ECW. Doses were generally well tolerated, but there was a decrease in Hb and a nondose-dependent decrease in Hct. There were also small decreases in DBP, but not SBP.*
L1351: ## **Study AC-080A201: Multi-Center, Double-Blind, Double-Dummy, Randomized, Placebo- and Active-Reference, Parallel Group, Phase 2 Dose-Finding Study With ACT-132577 in Subjects With Essential Hypertension (Grade 1 and 2)**
L1352: ## Design
L1353: This study was a prospective, multi-center, double-blind, double-dummy, randomized, placebo- and active-reference, parallel group, Phase 2, dose-finding study with aprocitentan administered in a capsule in subjects with essential hypertension (grade 1 and 2). The primary objective was to explore the dose response of aprocitentan on DBP in subjects with grade 1 or 2 essential hypertension. Secondary objectives included evaluation of the dose-response of aprocitentan on SBP, control and response rate of blood pressure, 24-hour ambulatory blood pressure monitoring (ABPM), and safety and tolerability. Other objectives included evaluation of the steady-state trough plasma concentrations of aprocitentan and possible metabolites in hypertensive subjects, evaluation of steady-state trough plasma concentrations of ET-1 in hypertensive subjects, and investigation of the correlation between ET-1 and aprocitentan plasma concentrations.
L1354: This study consisted of the following periods: prescreening, screening, run-in (Period I), treatment (Period II), withdrawal (Period III), follow-up, and end-of-study. The run-in period (Period I) was a single-blind period of 4 to 6 weeks. For antihypertensive treatment-naïve subjects, Period I lasted 4 weeks, and for subjects on antihypertensive treatment, Period I lasted 6 weeks, including a 2-week washout period for antihypertensive treatment. Period II was the double-blind treatment period of 8 weeks. Period III was the single-blind placebo period of 2 weeks where subjects were given a withdrawal placebo. 490 subjects were randomized in a 1:1:1:1:1:1 ratio into the following 6 groups: 4 aprocitentan dose groups (5, 10, 25, and 50 mg), 20 mg lisinopril, and placebo.
L1355: ## Results
L1356: Mean (SD) changes from baseline in mean trough sitting diastolic blood pressure (SiDBP) were -6.3 (8.9), -9.9 (8.7), -12 (8.2), and -10 (7.9) mmHg for the 5, 10, 25, and 50 mg aprocitentan dose groups respectively, -4.9 (11.1) mmHg for placebo, and -8.4 (9.6) mmHg for 20 mg lisinopril. Mean (SD) changes from baseline in mean trough sitting systolic blood pressure (SiSBP) were -10.3 (15.3), -15.0 (14.5), -18.5 (15.0) and -15.1 (11.8) mmHg in the 5, 10, 25 and 50 mg aprocitentan groups, respectively, compared with -7.7 (18.8) mmHg in the placebo group and -12.8 (16.0) mmHg in the 20 mg lisinopril group.
L1357: Mean (SD) changes from baseline to Week 8 in 24-h mean DBP were -3.76 (6.36), -6.55 (7.04), -8.86 (7.35) and -5.98 (6.63) mmHg in the 5, 10, 25 and 50 mg aprocitentan groups, respectively, compared with -2.49 (5.52) mmHg in the placebo group. The mean change from baseline for DBP in the 20 mg lisinopril group was -5.72 (9.12) mmHg. Mean (SD) changes from baseline to Week 8 in 24-h mean SBP were -3.16 (10.54), -7.72 (11.37), -9.23 (9.92) and -6.07 (8.93) mmHg in the 5, 10, 25 and 50 mg aprocitentan groups, respectively, compared with -3.54 (7.46) mmHg in the placebo group. The mean change from baseline in the 20 mg lisinopril group was -7.23 (14.33) mmHg.
L1358: Steady-state conditions were reached by Week 2 across all aprocitentan treatment groups, indicated by similar trough plasma concentrations at Weeks 2, 4, and 8.
L1359: ET-1 concentrations ranged from 1.84-1.97 pg/mL for the placebo group to 2.99-3.16 pg/mL for the 50 mg aprocitentan group.
L1360: A dose-dependent decrease in hemoglobin from baseline to Week 8 was seen for aprocitentan groups (range from -1.3 to -6.7 g/L) compared with a +2.2 g/L increase in the placebo group. There was no change in mean body weight for any aprocitentan dose group, which suggests that there may not have been significant fluid retention.
L1361: ## Agency Assessment
L1362: Generally, change from baseline to Week 8 in mean trough SiDBP decreased dose dependently up to 25 mg aprocitentan; however, the 50 mg dose of aprocitentan appears to have resulted in a smaller decrease in SiDBP than the 25 mg dose (-10 mmHg versus -12 mmHg, respectively). This trend was consistent with SiSBP results. Given that both the 10 mg and 50 mg aprocitentan doses appeared to decrease blood pressure to a similar extent, and the 25 mg dose appeared to have the greatest effect on blood pressure in this study, doses at or near 10 mg and 25 mg
L1363: seemed to be reasonable to explore in future studies. Two doses, a 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose, were selected for further study in the Phase 3 study (PRECISION).
L1364: Interestingly, the arithmetic mean C_{trough} values were lower (range of 1.57–1.85 \mug/mL at the different visits for the 25 mg dose; SD range of 1.27–1.43 \mug/mL), with greater variability based on SD than for observations in healthy subjects (mean C_{trough} range of 2.26–3.22 \mug/mL and SD range of 0.22–0.78 \mug/mL for the 25 mg dose in healthy subjects, including Japanese subjects or subjects on a high sodium diet). There does not appear to be an explanation for this difference between subjects in this study and healthy subjects. Of note, in the Phase 3 study (PRECISION) in subjects with difficult-to-control hypertension, the mean C_{trough} (SD) values measured at Week 4 were 1.31 (0.53) \mug/mL in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg group and 2.70 (1.16) \mug/mL in the aprocitentan 25 mg group. These C_{trough} concentrations were in the range of those observed in healthy subjects at similar/equivalent doses; therefore, it is unlikely that disease state affected the C_{trough} of aprocitentan.
L1365: ## **PRECISION: Multi-Center, Blinded, Randomized, Parallel-Group, Phase 3 Study With Aprocitentan in Subjects With Resistant Hypertension**
L1366: ## **Design**
L1367: Refer to Section 6.2.1.1 for details on the study design.
L1368: ## **Results**
L1369: Trough plasma concentrations of aprocitentan were measured at the end of Part 1. Median plasma trough concentrations were 1310 ng/mL (range: 111–3090 ng/mL; CV%: 40.4) in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg group and 2620 ng/mL (range: 39.1–8700 ng/mL; CV%: 43.1) in the aprocitentan 25 mg group.
L1370: Changes in blood pressure from baseline to Week 4 and from baseline of the double-blind (DB)-withdrawal (WD) to Week 40 for the primary and secondary endpoints are summarized in Table 103. Decreases in systolic blood pressure were similar for the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose between baseline and Week 4. During the DB-WD, SiSBP and SiDBP continued to decrease slightly in those on 25 mg aprocitentan and increase in those on placebo.
L1371: Changes in 24 hour mean SBP and DBP from baseline to Week 4 and from the DB-WD baseline to Week 40 are in Table 104. From baseline to Week 4, the 25 mg dose of aprocitentan decreased the mean 24 hour SBP and DBP more than the 12.5 mg dose. In the DB-WD period, those given placebo had increases in both 24 hour mean SBP and DBP. Changes in mean daytime and nighttime SBP and DBP from baseline to Week 4 and from the DB-WD baseline to Week 40 are in Table 105. From baseline to Week 4, the 25 mg dose of aprocitentan decreased the mean daytime and nighttime SBP and DBP more than the 12.5 mg dose. During the DB-WD period, the daytime and nighttime mean SBP and DBP increased for those on placebo. While the mean nighttime SBP increased by 0.26 mmHg between the DB-WD and Week 40, likely due to variability, the increase for those on placebo was much higher (mean increase of 8.76 mmHg).

L1397: ## Agency Assessment
L1398: *In the full population, the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose showed similar SiSBP and SiDBP lowering in the first 4 weeks in the DB treatment period. ABPM parameters showed a mean greater improvement with the 25 mg dose compared to the 12.5 mg dose. The extent of the benefit of the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose varied in different populations (i.e., different renal function status and race). In the DB-WD period, those remaining on 25 mg aprocitentan maintained lower mean blood pressure measurements compared to those who switched to placebo.*
L1399: At the request of the review team, the Applicant sent in additional analysis in documents titled "Response to 4 May 2023 Information Request" (document number D-23.205), "Response to 30 May 2023 Information Request" (document number D-23.226), and "Response to 6 June 2023 Information Request" (document number D-23.234). These are discussed further in the section below on Assessment of Dose and Potential effectiveness.
L1400: ## **Assessment of Dose and Potential Effectiveness**
L1401: Per the proposed labeling, the recommended (b) (4) dose of aprocitentan is 12.5 mg orally once daily, (b) (4). The proposed dose (b) (4) based on the results of the Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies. After reviewing the data, it appears that (b) (4) the 12.5 mg (b) (4) dose provide benefit based on the primary endpoint compared to placebo; (b) (4). ABPM data suggests that there may be some improvement between the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose; however, the improvement is minimal. Details of dose response analysis are discussed below.
L1402: In the Phase 2 study (Study AC-080A201), aprocitentan doses of 10, 25, and 50 mg decreased sitting systolic/diastolic unattended automated office blood pressure from baseline to Week 8, with placebo-corrected reductions of 7.1/4.9, 9.9/7.0 and 7.6/5.0 mmHg, respectively, compared with a reduction of 4.8/3.8 mmHg with lisinopril 20 mg. For those with valid ambulatory blood pressure readings, aprocitentan 10, 25, and 50 mg significantly decreased placebo-corrected 24-hour systolic/diastolic blood pressure by 4.0/4.0, 4.8/5.9, and 3.7/4.5 mmHg, respectively.
L1403: The doses for the Phase 3 study (PRECISION) were based on data from the Phase 2 study and the PD Phase 1 study, AC-080-102, which evaluated the effect of aprocitentan on body weight in healthy male subjects on a high-sodium diet. While noninferiority versus placebo in change from baseline to Day 9 body weight could not be demonstrated for the 25 and 50 mg doses of aprocitentan, only the 50 mg dose significantly increased body weight compared to placebo (p=0.017). Taken together, the Applicant selected a 12.5 mg and 25 mg once daily dose for the Phase 3 study, believing that aprocitentan in the range of 10 to 25 mg could provide the best risk/benefit profile.
L1404: In the PRECISION study, the mean (\pm SE) change in SBP at 4 weeks was -15.3\pm 0.9 mmHg for aprocitentan 12.5 mg, -15.2\pm 0.9 mmHg for 25 mg, and -11.5\pm 0.9 mmHg for placebo, for a difference versus placebo of -3.8\pm 1.3 mmHg (97.5% CI: -6.8, -0.8; p=0.004) and -3.7\pm 1.3 mmHg (97.5% CI: -6.7, -0.8; p=0.005), respectively. Based on the data for the primary efficacy endpoint, it is unclear if the 25 mg dose would provide any additional benefit compared to placebo. Differences between aprocitentan 25 mg and 12.5 mg for various blood pressure endpoints are included in Table 108 below. Exposure-response analyses for all blood pressure endpoints are included in Table 109.

L1416: Subgroup analyses were also completed for Black or African Americans (Table 110), subjects with stage 3-4 chronic kidney disease (Table 111), the population without Black or African Americans or those with chronic kidney disease stage 3-4 (Table 112), and subjects 75 years and older (Table 113). Per the Applicant's analysis, an improvement in the primary blood pressure endpoint was seen in those with stage 3 and 4 CKD. Subgroup analyses by baseline CKD stage showed that the heterogeneity observed for CKD stages 1-2 compared to CKD stages 3-4 for the 25 mg dose was likely due to random chance (refer to subgroup analyses in Section 6.2.1.4 for details). Additionally, per the Applicant, there was a larger decrease in the primary blood pressure endpoint in the ≥75-year-old population with the 25 mg dose compared to the 12.5 mg dose.

L1445: In a further attempt to determine if there is any additional benefit with the 25 mg dose compared to the 12.5 mg dose, the change in blood pressure endpoints from Week 4 (end of Part 1) to Week 36 (end of Part 2) for those randomized to 12.5 mg in Part 1 was assessed (Table 114). The data at a single timepoint for the change in SiSBP for each subject from Week 4 to Week 36 is plotted in Figure 18. The data in Figure 18 shows that while many subjects appeared to have derived further benefit with a decrease in SiSBP from an increased aprocitentan dose, many other subjects did not.
L1446: Because SiSBP readings in the office can be variable, 24 hour mean SBP measured by ABPM may be a better measure of change in systolic blood pressure. The data for the change in 24 hour mean SBP measured by ABPM between Week 4 and Week 36 is presented in Figure 19. Similar to SiSBP, there were many subjects that had a decrease in 24 hour mean SBP measured by ABPM from an increased aprocitentan dose, though many others did not. The mean difference in 24 hour SBP measured by ABPM was -0.5 mmHg, and as seen in Figure 19, there are a few individuals who appear to have much larger changes in blood pressure.

L1458: To evaluate if the duration of treatment alone may have an impact on BP, the change in blood pressure endpoints from Week 4 (end of Part 1) to Week 36 (end of Part 2) for those randomized to 25 mg in Part 1 (and remained on 25 mg dose in Part 2) was also assessed (Table 115). The data for the change in SiSBP for each individual from Week 4 to Week 36 is plotted in Figure 20. Similar to the data in Figure 18, the data in Figure 20 shows that while many subjects had a decrease in SiSBP from maintaining a 25 mg aprocitentan dose, many other subjects did not. The mean difference in SiSBP was -0.84 mmHg, and as seen in Figure 20, there are several individuals who appear to have much larger changes in blood pressure. Therefore, it does not appear that there was an additional large decrease in blood pressure from Week 4 to Week 36 in those receiving a 25 mg aprocitentan dose. The decrease that is seen in SiSBP could be due to variability. Similar to SiSBP, there were many subjects that had decreased 24 hour mean SBP measured by ABPM when remaining on a 25 mg dose, though many others did not. The mean difference in 24-hour SBP measured by ABPM was 1.32 mmHg, and as seen in Figure 21, there are a few individuals who appear to have much larger changes in blood pressure.

L1477: ## 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1478: ## Bioanalytical Method for Aprocitentan
L1479: Bioanalytical method METH-051 was developed for detecting aprocitentan in plasma via liquid chromatography with tandem mass spectrometry. A full method validation was completed, then in-study validation was conducted for each clinical study. Bioanalytical lifecycle information is included in Table 118, and a summary of the method and method performance is in Table 119.

L1498: ## **Bioanalytical Method for Midazolam**
L1499: A method for determining midazolam and 1-hydroxy midazolam in plasma samples was developed and validated by [REDACTED](b) (4) Project-No.: 079B16-Val). Results of the validation are presented in Table 120 for midazolam and Table 121 for 1-hydroxymidazolam. The assay is adequately validated, and long-term stability in plasma was documented out to 135 days and 181 days at ≤ -70 °C and ≤ -20 °C, respectively.
L1500: Table 120. Summary of Validation Data for Midazolam
L1501: Parameters	Results	Acceptance criteria
Selectivity for midazolam
Investigation of interference caused by blank plasma (six different plasma sources)	No interference	The chromatograms should show no interfering peak at retention time of midazolam. If there is a peak present, the area should be < 20 % of the mean peak area of midazolam at LLOQ.
Investigation of interference caused by haemolysed blank plasma and hyperlipidaemic blank plasma	Haemolysed blank plasma and hyperlipidaemic blank plasma caused no interfering peak
Investigation of interference caused by further analytes, metabolites and internal standards	Both internal standards caused no interfering peak; 1-hydroxymidazolam and 4-hydroxymidazolam showed less than 20 % interference compared to response of LLOQ
Investigation of interference caused by co-medication	Co-medication caused no interfering peak	Acceptance criteria were fulfilled
Selectivity for internal standard midazolam-d4
Investigation of interference caused by blank plasma (six different plasma sources)	No interference	The chromatograms should show no interfering peak at retention time of midazolam-d4. If there is a peak present, the area should be < 5 % of the mean peak area of the internal standard in LLOQ samples.
Investigation of interference caused by haemolysed blank plasma and hyperlipidaemic blank plasma	Haemolysed blank plasma and hyperlipidaemic blank plasma caused no interfering peak
Investigation of interference caused by the analytes, metabolites and further internal standard	1-Hydroxymidazolam, 1-hydroxymidazolam-d4 and 4-hydroxymidazolam caused no interfering peak; midazolam showed less than 5 % interference compared to response of internal standard in LLOQ samples
Investigation of interference caused by co-medication	Co-medication caused no interfering peak	Acceptance criteria were fulfilled
Carryover	Response in blank samples after ULOQ showed less than 20 % interference at retention time of midazolam and less than 5 % interference at retention time of its internal standard compared to the respective response in LLOQ samples. L1518: ## Bioanalytical Method for ^{14}\text{C} Radioactivity
L1519: A method for determining ^{14}\text{C} radioactivity in human plasma by liquid scintillation counting was developed and validated by (b) (4) -024033/ (b) (4) -RD-559). The method had a 3-run validation, and the method was considered validated if the within-run, between-run, and overall precision were \le 15\% (20% at the LLQ), and if the bias was between -15% and 15% (between -20% and 20% at the LLQ). The within-run, between-run, and overall coefficients of variation were not higher than 19% at LLQ and not higher than 5% at other QC levels. The overall bias of the heparin plasma method for the 1.00, 0.500, and 0.250 mL assay was between 5.2% and 16.3%. For hemolytic heparin plasma, the accuracy and precision results met the criteria except for the hemolytic samples at the LLQ, which showed an overall CV% &gt;20\% for the 1.00 mL assay and a bias &gt;20\% for the 0.250 mL assay. It was noted that hemolysis does not normally occur during plasma sample preparation, but if it were to occur, a high CV% or high bias is only observed at the LLQ, indicating that hemolysis generally has a low impact on the study results. The study also showed that both heparin and EDTA could be used as an anticoagulant, as the results for experiments measuring the effect of EDTA plasma samples did not deviate by more than 15% from the mean activity in heparin plasma samples for all 5 activity levels. One deviation occurred: for plasma, the quench curve used for efficiency corrections was not a ^{14}\text{C} low level quenched standard set, but an in-house prepared quench curve was made. It was noted that the use of the in-house prepared quench curve significantly improved the assay method. In an amendment (b) (4) -034523), a 2 mL assay was added. For
L1520: heparin plasma, the within-run, between-run, and overall CV were all not higher than 11.4% for all ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity levels. The overall bias was between 9.0% and 11.7%. For hemolytic plasma, the within-run, between-run, and overall CV were all not higher than 17.1% for all ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity levels, and the overall bias was between -7.6% and +7.8%.
L1521: The validation of a method for the determination of total ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in human whole blood samples was determined at [REDACTED] (b) (4) (Method Number [REDACTED] (b) (4) -RD-1360). The method used liquid scintillation counting using low level counting. The validation range was 50.0 to 20000 dpm/mL for total ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity. The matrix was human whole blood. Anticoagulants used included \text{K}_2\text{-EDTA}, \text{K}_3\text{-EDTA}, lithium heparin, and sodium heparin. The accuracy ranged from -4.6% to 14.5%, the precision ranged from 1.3% to 12.0%, the dilution as 10-fold and analysis was up to 200000 dpm/mL, and matrix variability was within criteria.
L1522: The validation of a method for determination of ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in human urine by liquid scintillation counting was determined at [REDACTED] (b) (4) -024033/ [REDACTED] (b) (4) -RD-562). The method was selective for quenching of endogenous urine compounds for the 1.00 mL assay. The selectivity for the 2.0 mL urine assay was not within its specifications because the bias of the assay was &gt;20% (i.e., 26.1%); however, it is expected that the high bias was due to an add-up of measurement errors because each sample is corrected for its own blank and the 6 independent samples were spiked at LLQ level. Because possible differences in transformed spectral index external standard (tSIE) values are corrected by the quench curve, the Applicant concluded that these results should be accepted. The method was considered validated if the within-run, between-run, and overall precision were \le 15\% (20% at the LLQ), and if the bias was between -15% and 15% (between -20% and 20% at the LLQ). Within-run, between-run, and overall CV were \le 16.3\% for all ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity levels. The overall bias of the urine method was between 0.3% and 16.6%. For the 2.00 mL urine assay, the within-run, between-run, and overall CV were \le 18.1\% for all ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity levels. The overall bias was between 3.6% and 15.8%. One deviation that occurred was that for urine, an in-house quench curve was used and significantly improved the assay method for determining the low level ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in human urine. The validation of a method for determination of ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in human feces by combustion and subsequent liquid scintillation counting was determined by [REDACTED] (b) (4) -034723/ [REDACTED] (b) (4) -RD-605). The method was considered to be valid if within-run, between-run and overall precision were \le 15\% (20% at the LLQ level) and if the bias was between -15% and 15% (between -20% and 20% at the LLQ). For feces homogenized in 2% Arabic gum, the validated range was 50.0 dpm.\text{g}^{-1} – 1000000 dpm.\text{g}^{-1}. Within-run, between-run, and overall CV were not higher than 14.7% for all ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity levels in the validated range. Overall bias was between -6.3% to 8.6%. For feces homogenized in water, the validated range was 40.0 dpm.\text{g}^{-1} – 1000000 dpm.\text{g}^{-1}. Within-run, between-run, and overall CV were not higher than 19.3% for all ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity levels. Overall bias was between -12.6% to 4.0%.
L1523: An addendum was completed for the determination of ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in human urine and feces by [REDACTED] (b) (4) -RD-562 / [REDACTED] (b) (4) -RD-605 / [REDACTED] (b) (4) -044173, S310.1.005) for the partial validation of a method for determination of ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in stabilized urine and feces samples. For precision, within-run, between-run, and total CV were calculated at each level, and the total CV could not exceed 15% (20% at the lower limit of quantification [LLOQ]). For accuracy, the overall bias was calculated at each level, and bias could not exceed \pm 15\% (20% at LLOQ). The methods for determination of ^{14}\text{C}-radioactivity in
L1524: human urine in the presence of citric acid and human feces in the presence of 2 M sodium acetate were validated successfully.

L1527: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1528: ## 14.5.1. Executive Summary
L1529: The Applicant is seeking approval of aprocitentan, a dual ERA, for the treatment of hypertension in subjects who are not adequately controlled on other drugs. A population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) model was developed using data from 902 subjects receiving active treatment, including data from 251 subjects in Phase 1 studies, 226 subjects with essential hypertension in Phase 2, and 425 subjects with resistant hypertension in the Applicant's Phase 3 studies. The key pharmacometrics findings are summarized below.
L1530: - There does not appear to be a difference in efficacy between the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose, as demonstrated by the flat exposure-response (E-R) relationship for the change from baseline in SiSBP11 (see Figure 22).(b) (4)
L1531: 11 SiSBP was measured by unattended automatic office blood pressure measurement (uAOBPM), page 36 (link)

L1546: ## 14.5.2. Population PK Analysis
L1547: ## 14.5.2.1. Review Summary
L1548: The Applicant's PopPK analysis for apocitentan is acceptable to support the current submission. The Applicant's final PopPK model adequately described the apocitentan concentrations, in which PopPK parameter estimates were estimated with acceptable precision as indicated by the relative standard errors (RSE) for total body clearance of drug (CL, 1.9% RSE), volume of distribution in central compartment (Vc, 0.4% RSE), volume of distribution in peripheral compartment (Vp, 2.4% RSE), intercompartment clearance (Q, 16.6% RSE) and Ka (7.3% RSE). The goodness-of-fit plots showed good agreement between the observed and the individual predicted concentrations without any obvious bias over time. The prediction-corrected visual predictive check plots showed good agreement between the observed and the simulated concentrations. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. For further comments on the utility of the model to support labeling and derive exposure metrics for exposure-response analyses, see Table 125.
L1549: **Table 125. Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK Model**
L1550: Utility of the Final Model	Reviewer's Comments
Utility to support Applicant's proposed labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factors	Intrinsic factor	Based on a PopPK analysis, renal impairment, and moderate hepatic impairment do not have a clinically meaningful effect on the PK. Dose adjustment based on renal impairment and hepatic impairment is not required.
Body weight was identified as a statistically significant covariate for CL, Vc, Vp, Q.
Food effect was identified as a statistically significant covariate for absorption lag time and Ka.
Extrinsic factor	NA

L1553: ## 14.5.2.2. Introduction
L1554: The primary objectives of the Applicant's PopPK analysis were to:
L1555: - Develop a population PK model describing aprocitentan plasma concentration over time at the population level and at the individual level.
- Investigate the relationship between covariates and model parameters.
- Quantify and visualize the influence of identified covariates on the concentration-time profile of aprocitentan via model-based simulations, enabling judgment about the clinical relevance of the covariates.
L1556: ## 14.5.2.3. PopPK Model Development
L1557: ## Data
L1558: A total of 902 subjects receiving active treatment were included in the PK modeling data set, of which 251 subjects were enrolled in Phase 1 studies, 226 were enrolled in a Phase 2 study conducted in subjects with essential hypertension, and 425 were enrolled in a Phase 3 study conducted in subjects with resistant hypertension. For an overview of the studies, see Table 126.
L1559: A total of 356 out of 8450 (4.2%) aprocitentan concentration measurements in the modeling dataset were below the limit of quantification; most of these measurements were predose measurements obtained in Phase 1 studies.12 Measurements below the limit of quantification were treated by M4 method, which was set to the LLOQ of 5 ng/mL and treated as censored in the PopPK analysis. Predose PK measurements obtained prior to first study treatment administration were set to a value of 0.05 ng/mL (censored) and specified as a missing dependent variable in the modeling data set (i.e., not used for modeling).
L1560: In addition to the 8450 (94.4%) records, a total of 498 records (5.6%) were kept in the data set but flagged for exclusion for various reasons, such as extreme low values, missed dosing time, missed sampling time, etc.13
L1561: ## Agency Assessment
L1562: *The Applicant excluded observations with extremely low values, which may affect parameter estimates of PopPK models. Model parameter estimates based on the inclusion of extremely low values and creatinine imputation were similar to those of the Applicant's final PopPK model. Therefore, the Applicant's final PopPK model is considered acceptable.*

L1564: 13 Applicant's poppk report (link), Page 20.

L1586: ## Base Model
L1587: The base model is a two-compartment PK model with absorption lag time (flag), first-order absorption and first-order elimination from the central compartment.
L1588: Interindividual variability was modelled assuming a log-normal distribution for subject level random effects. The interindividual variability was considered for total body clearance of drug (CL), volume of distribution for central compartment (Vc), tlag, absorption rate constant (ka), volume of distribution of peripheral compartment (Vp) and intercompartmental drug transfer (Q).
L1589: Intra-individual variability was tested as combined additive and proportional error model on the dependent variable.
L1590: Model evaluation and selection were based on the point estimates of PK parameters, their respective relative standard errors and standard statistical criteria of goodness-of-fit such as a decrease in the minimum objective function value, successful model convergence, and diagnostic prediction-corrected visual predictive check.
L1591: ## Covariate Analysis
L1592: Covariate parameters such as body weight, sex, age, formulation, race, etc. shown in Table 128, were investigated.
L1593: ## Agency Assessment
L1594: Per FDA General Clinical Pharmacology Considerations for Pediatric Studies for Drugs and Biological Product (Wang et al. 2012; September 2022), there are enough samples for subjects in renal groups and hepatic groups to target a 95% CI [confidence interval] of the geometric mean estimates of clearance and volume of distribution in each subgroup with at least 80% power.

L1603: ## **Software and Estimation Methods**
L1604: Population PK modeling was performed using nonlinear mixed effects modeling techniques implemented in Monolix version 2021R1 (Lixoft 2021a). The stochastic approximation expectation-maximization (SAEM) algorithm was used for parameter estimation (Delyon et al. 1999). Exploratory graphics were created in R version 4.04. Simulations were performed with Simulx version 2021R1 (Lixoft 2021b) and Berkeley Madonna (Marcoline et al. 2022).
L1605: ## **14.5.2.4. Final Model**
L1606: The parameter estimates for the final PopPK model are listed in Table 129. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final model are shown in Figure 24. The prediction-corrected visual predictive check plot for the final covariate model with all data is shown in Figure 25 (Stratified by dose), and estimate of interindividual variability and conditional weighted residual histograms for the final PopPK model are shown in Figure 26.
L1607: The final PopPK model was a 2-compartmental model as parameterized with Tlag, CL, V2, V3, Q. An exponential model was used for interindividual variability, and a combined additive and proportional error model was used for intra-individual variability.
L1608: - Relative bioavailability (F) was 21.7% and 43.6% lower after administration of 300 and 600 mg, respectively, compared to doses of up to 100 mg.
- Food status was included as a covariate for absorption parameters tlag and ka.
- Clearance and volume parameters were found to increase with body weight, and clearance increased with higher eGFR, decreased with moderate hepatic impairment (HI), and was slightly higher in male subjects compared to female subjects.
- Aprocitentan clearance for a reference female subject versus a male reference subject with body weight of 87 kg (median in the modeling data set), no HI, and eGFR of 90 mL/min/1.73 m2 was 0.342 versus 0.354 L/h, respectively.

L1626: ## Agency Assessment
L1627: The reviewer independently assessed covariates of the final model by Monolix (version 2021R1). HI and eGFR were not identified as significant covariates, which implies that dose adjustment for moderate hepatic impairment and renal impairment are not required. In contrast, formulation (capsule, tablet A, tablet B) was identified as a significant covariate as shown in Table 130. The optimized model parameter estimates are shown in Table 131. Model parameter estimates based on this model were, as a whole, similar to those obtained in the Applicant's final model. Therefore, the Applicant's final model is considered acceptable.

L1650: ## Agency Assessment
L1651: The Applicant's simulation did not consider intersubject variability, which is required to predict individual exposures. Therefore, the reviewer independently simulated individual exposures with intersubject variability, which is used to assess exposures of patients with renal impairment, patients with hepatic impairment, and formulations. AUCss was calculated based on F1, CL and dose, in which doses (300 mg and 600 mg) were excluded for their lower F1 values compared to doses ≤100 mg. In order to summarize the AUCss under different groups, the AUCss was normalized by AUCss/dose. The normalized AUCss ratios among eGFR, shown in Table 133, are similar to those obtained via the Applicant's evaluation, in which eGFR (90 mL/min/1.73 m2) was treated as a reference. The normalized AUCss ratio between moderate hepatic impairment and healthy subjects, shown in Table 134, is a little higher than that of the Applicant's simulation (130 versus 122). The normalized AUCss ratios among formulations (capsule, tablet A, tablet B) are shown in Table 135. As shown in the table, normalized AUCss of the tablet A and tablet B have similar geometric means, while the normalized AUCss of the capsule has a lower geometric mean.

L1665: ## Agency Assessment
L1666: Based on the model simulation, the highest difference among the normalized AUCss ratios assessment among groups (moderate hepatic impairment versus healthy, renal impairment versus healthy) is 35%, which is not clinically meaningful. Therefore, no dose adjustment is needed for patients with renal impairment (eGFR &gt;15 mL/min/1.73 m2) and patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
L1667: ## 14.5.3. Exposure-Response Relationship
L1668: The primary efficacy endpoint in the Phase 3 study (PRECISION)14 was the change from baseline at Week 4 of the double-blind treatment period in mean trough SiSBP. The reviewer conducted an E-R analysis between AUCss (estimated for the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose based on the PopPK model) and the change from baseline in SiSBP at Week 4 in the Phase 3 study. As shown in Figure 27, the E-R relationship appears to be flat over the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose range and the p-value (0.176) of AUCss slope is not statistically significant. The relationship between race and response (change from baseline in SiSBP at week 4) was also assessed and is shown in Figure 28. As indicated by the boxplots, there does not appear to be an obvious difference in median response between the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose in subjects who are Black or African American.

L1678: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1679: ## 15.1. Important Study Dates
L1680: The study enrolled its first subject on June 18, 2018, and the last subject had their last visit on April 25, 2022. The database lock for PRECISION study occurred on May 12, 2022.
L1681: ## 15.2. Protocol Amendments
L1682: The clinical protocol was amended globally twice. An overview of these amendments is provided in the table below.
L1683: **Table 136. Overview of Integrated Protocol Amendments, PRECISION Study**
L1684: Amendment	Subjects Randomized
n (%)	Summary of Significant Changes
Amendment 1
(protocol version 2), September 19, 2018.	286 (39%)	
Exclusion of subjects with severe hepatic impairment added to exclusion criterion 16.
Update to Section 4.5.2, acceptable methods of contraception, to comply with the Clinical Trials Facilitation Group recommendations related to contraception and pregnancy testing in clinical trials requiring a highly effective contraception method.
Therefore, option 3 (combination of diaphragm with spermicide or cervical cap with spermicide with male condom) was removed from Section 4.5.2.
Update to Section 5.2.3.2 (diuretics for fluid retention) to provide more guidance for diagnosis of fluid retention cases requiring treatment with diuretics.
Added creatine kinase to the blood chemistry parameters for monitoring of potential myopathy/rhabdomyolysis.
Exclusion criterion 5: Added atrial fibrillation to this criterion. This criterion was mistakenly missed in protocol Version 1. It ensures an accurate recording of ambulatory blood pressure monitoring.
Exclusion criterion 16: Increased the NT-proBNP threshold from 200 pg/mL to 500 pg/mL. (It became clear, during the initial experience of Screening, that a large proportion of subjects with resistant hypertension (RHT) have values of NT-proBNP exceeding 200 pg/mL with no evidence of frailty, serious cardiac dysfunction, or heart failure.
Exclusion criterion 18: The NT-proBNP exclusion criterion 18 at Randomization has been removed since it is not expected that the medical condition of subjects who fulfill all inclusion/exclusion criteria, and are successfully entered in the study, will deteriorate between Screening and Randomization.

Amendment 2
(protocol version 3), February 27, 2020.	420 (58)	
Changed inclusion criteria 4 to not require a diuretic as one of the three antihypertensive classes for enrollment. This change was implemented to improve recruitment. Subjects were still placed on diuretic therapy as part of SBAT.
Changed inclusion criteria 11 to allow for stable dose of SBAT for at least one week before the end of the RI period (previously stable for entire RI period)
L1685: Amendment	Subjects Randomized
n (%)	Summary of Significant Changes
Expanded exclusion criteria 5 to include clinically significant unstable cardiac disease at screening or in the past and expanded definition of congestive heart failure to include NYHA stage II with relevant mitral valve insufficiency and/or aortic stenosis.
Exclusion criteria 11 (Treatment with any medication which may affect BP) was expanded to also exclude treatment with high dose of loop diuretics (i.e., furosemide greater than 80 mg/day, or equivalent dosage of other loop diuretics).
The second component of the study-specific discontinuation criterion for renal failure was modified from \ge 30\% decreased eGFR to 2X increase in serum creatinine to avoid discontinuation of subjects due to potentially transient eGFR reduction.
Removal of erythropoiesis-stimulating agents and psychiatric drugs from the list of the forbidden medications and adding them to allowed medications with the provision that they have been initiated and the dose has been stable at least 4 weeks prior to the screening visit, and that the dose is kept stable until the end of treatment.

Global COVID-19 protocol amendment
April 8, 2020	451 (62)	A global protocol addendum was issued to address the conduct of the study during the COVID-19 pandemic: measures and instructions, in line with health authority guidelines, were introduced to preserve subject safety, trial integrity and interpretability, and compliance with regulatory requirements.

L1687: Abbreviations: BP, blood pressure; COVID-19, coronavirus disease 2019; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; n, number of subjects; NT-proBNP, N-terminal pro-brain natriuretic peptide; NYHA, New York Heart Association; RHT, resistant hypertension; RI, run-in; please standard background antihypertensive therapy
L1688: ## 15.3. Study Administrative Structure
L1689: ## Independent Data Monitoring Committee
L1690: The independent data monitoring committee (IDMC) was composed of 4 external experts with experience and expertise in the cardiovascular domain, statistical methodologies, safety monitoring, and overseeing randomized clinical studies. The IDMC was fully operational prior to enrollment of the first subject in the study. The IDMC had overall responsibility for safeguarding the interests of subjects by monitoring unblinded safety and efficacy data obtained in the study and making appropriate recommendations based on the reported data, thus ensuring that the study was conducted to the highest scientific and ethical standards. Unblinded data were delivered to the IDMC by an Independent Statistical Analysis Center. The details of the IDMC organization, roles, and responsibilities, and conduct of meetings were defined in the IDMC charter.
L1691: Each meeting begins with an open session that Applicant representatives and coordinating investigators could attend. Data presented in the open session could include recruitment status, clinical study conduct (e.g., frequency of protocol deviations, withdrawal of consent from study, lost to follow-up, subject discontinuation, list of serious adverse events [AEs]), protocol amendments, communications with Health Authorities and any other metrics if needed. This was followed by a closed session, in which the IDMC discussed the unblinded results. A member of the Independent Statistical Analysis Center attended the closed session meeting (e.g., to present results and answer questions).
L1692: IDMC met nine times over the course of the study and conducted nine unblinded data reviews. Following evaluation of subject data during the third IDMC meeting (February 3, 2020), the IDMC recommended that subjects receiving treatment with high doses of loop diuretics be excluded from participation in the study. A letter was sent to the investigators on February 19, 2020 to exclude subjects with high doses of loop diuretics and exclusion criterion #11 was amended accordingly (*Global Protocol Amendment 2*).
L1693: See Section 15.5 for information on the administrative structure as relates to preparing the NDA submission and/or blinding related issues.
L1694: ## Independent Central Adjudication Committee
L1695: The CAC was composed of 3 external experts with experience in cardiovascular / renal medicine and expertise in the assessment of clinical events within a study. The CAC members were responsible for adjudicating, in a standardized and blinded fashion, all AEs/serious AEs identified by the Investigator or Sponsor which indicated potential major adverse cardiovascular events (MACE)-plus. MACE was defined as cardiovascular death, nonfatal myocardial infarction, or nonfatal stroke. MACE-plus included, in addition, hospitalization for heart failure. The details of the CAC organization, roles and responsibilities, and adjudication process were described in the CAC charter.
L1696: ## 15.4. Study Assessments
L1697: ## Baseline Values
L1698: All baseline blood pressure and laboratory measurements were collected during the run-in period (Day -1). Baseline is defined as the last available measurement before or on the day of randomization. DB-WD baseline (Week 36) is defined as the last available measurement before or on the day of rerandomization. If both date and time of the measurement are collected, baseline is the last measurement up to the date and time of randomization. If only the date is collected, baseline is the last measurement before the date of randomization.
L1699: ## uAOBPM
L1700: An automatic oscillometric sphygmomanometer was provided to each site by the vendor and had to be managed by qualified personnel. Wherever possible, the same person used the device for a given subject at each visit. Sitting BP, standing BP, and pulse rate were measured noninvasively throughout the study using this device, as described below.
L1701: BP was required to be measured at trough (i.e., before taking study treatment and standard background antihypertensive therapy [SBAT]), at around the same clock time, at each visit. Measurements were to be recorded from the same arm of the subject each time, using the appropriate cuff size as determined during screening.
L1702: Details on the blood pressure procedure, including the device to be used, subject preparation (e.g., arm selection, arm position, cuff size) were detailed in the BP laboratory manual and followed the American Heart Association guidelines/Canadian Education Program on Hypertension (Pickering et al. 2005; Daskalopoulou et al. 2012). Automated office blood pressure measurement data was electronically transferred to the central BP laboratory and subsequently to the Applicant.
L1703: Sitting BP: The uAOBPM device recorded 5 BP readings (SiSBP and SiDBP), with preset time intervals between them. The first reading was taken after the subject had been sitting undisturbed in a quiet place for 5 minutes; this reading was excluded, and the device generated an average (mean trough) of the remaining four readings, taken at 1 minute intervals, for each variable.
L1704: Standing BP: monitoring of orthostatic hypotension was performed by measuring SBP and DBP twice, approximately 1 and 3 minutes after standing.
L1705: Pulse rate: measured at each visit at the same time as BP, while sitting. The last value recorded was used for analysis.
L1706: ## **ABPM**
L1707: ABPM devices were provided to each site by the central vendor. Subjects were required to attend the site on 2 consecutive days for each of the following visits:
L1708: - Baseline – End of the placebo run-in (RI) period; Visit 4 / Day –1
- End of DB Part 1 – Visit 6 / Week 4 (Day 28\pm 2 d)
- End of single-blind (SB) Part 2 – Visit 11 / Week 36 (Day 252\pm 12 d)
- During DB-WD Part 3 – Visit 13 / Week 40 (Day 280\pm 4 d)
L1709: On the first day, after all visit assessments had been performed, the ABPM device was applied to the subject who then took the study treatment and SBAT in the presence of study personnel (i.e., direct observed treatment). The following day, the subject returned to the site to have the ABPM device removed. The ABPM device was automatically programmed to inflate every 20 minutes during the day (06:00–21:59) and every 30 minutes during the night (22:00–05:59), resulting in 64 readings over a 24 h period (48 during the day, 16 during the night). The main evaluation of ABPM data was performed based on recordings with at least 70% valid readings and a total duration of at least 21 h.
L1710: ## **Home BP Measurement**
L1711: HBPM devices were provided to each site and could be lent to subjects to monitor their own BP at home throughout the study, as per the Investigator's judgment. Subjects who were given an HBPM device were instructed on its use; and informed that they had to measure their BP in the event of any severe symptoms related to hypertension (headache, vomiting, or nosebleeds) or orthostatic hypotension (light-headedness, presyncope, or dizziness).
L1712: Subjects were required to record their data (SBP/DBP values, time and date of measurement, and time of study treatment intake) on the card provided with the HBPM device, for 3 days in a row. If symptoms were still present on the third day, subjects were instructed to contact the study site.
L1713: Data recorded by subjects at home were filed in hospital charts only, not in the electronic case report form (eCRF) and were not provided to the Applicant.
L1714: During the COVID-19 pandemic, if subjects were not allowed or not willing to travel to the investigator site, remote visits (i.e., phone/video calls or home visits) were conducted. Subjects were requested to measure their BP on the day before the scheduled remote visit, using either the HBPM device provided to them for the study or their own BP device. As described above, subjects were required to record their BP data on the HBPM card, or in a notebook (source document), and then to provide these values to the study site staff during the remote visit. The data were recorded in the subject's medical chart, verified against the source at the next on-site visit, and entered in the eCRF.
L1715: ## Schedule of Assessments
L1716: ## Screening
L1717: The screening period lasted up to 12 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained: demographics/medical history, physical examination, concomitant medications, height/weight, 12-lead electrocardiogram (ECG), hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for urine albumin-to-creatinine ratio [UACR]), and uAOBPM.
L1718: ## Run-In
L1719: The run-in period lasted 4 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained: physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM.
L1720: ## DB Treatment
L1721: The DB treatment period lasted for 4 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained: physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM (Week 4).
L1722: ## SB Treatment
L1723: The SB treatment period lasted for 32 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained: physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM (Week 36). During the SB apocitentan part, urine pregnancy tests were performed monthly (i.e., between scheduled visits) at home with kits provided to the subject.
L1724: ## DB-WD Treatment
L1725: The DB-WD treatment period lasted for 12 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained: physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM (Week 40).
L1726: ## Follow-Up
L1727: Following completion of the treatment period, subjects entered the 30-day follow-up period where study medication will be discontinued, except for SBAT. The following assessments were obtained during the follow-up period: physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR), and uAOBPM.
L1728: **Table 137. Laboratory Parameters, PRECISION Study**

L1731: A central laboratory was used for all protocol-mandated hematology, clinical chemistry, and urinalysis tests, including retests due to laboratory abnormalities and tests performed at unscheduled visits. Two samples were drawn at the end of the placebo RI period (Day -1): One for the central laboratory, and one for a local laboratory. Eligibility for randomization was determined based on local laboratory results (hemoglobin, creatinine for eGFR, AST/ALT, and pregnancy test), which were recorded in the eCRF.

L1733: ## 15.5. Study Procedures
L1734: ## Randomization
L1735: Randomization was performed through an interactive response technology (IRT). For each part of the study, the investigator/delegate contacted the IRT system for allocation of subject numbers, treatment bottle numbers, and subject randomization/rerandomization numbers, as described below.
L1736: The IRT system was handled by an independent vendor which generated two randomization lists, one for DB Part 1 and one for DB-WD Part 3. At Visit 1 (Screening), after a subject had signed their ICF, the IRT system allocated a 7-digit subject number, which differed from the 5-digit randomization number. For subjects who were rescreened, a new subject number was assigned.
L1737: ## **Screening**
L1738: During the screening period, a subject's individual background antihypertensive medication (except beta-blockers, if present) was standardized by switching to SBAT, which was assigned via the IRT system. At the first run-in visit, the IRT system assigned the treatment bottle number for the 4-week placebo RI period.
L1739: ## **DB Treatment**
L1740: At Visit 4 / Randomization (Day 1), the IRT system randomized the subject to aprocitentan 12.5 mg, aprocitentan 25 mg, or placebo, in a 1:1:1 ratio. The IRT assigned a randomization number to the subject and a bottle number which matched the treatment arm as assigned on the randomization list.
L1741: ## **SB Treatment**
L1742: At the end of DB Part 1 (Week 4), the IRT system assigned the first treatment bottle numbers for the 32-week SB Part 2 (25 mg). At each subsequent visit when single-blind treatment was dispensed, the IRT assigned the treatment bottle numbers.
L1743: ## **DB-WD Treatment**
L1744: At the end of SB Part 2 (Week 36), the IRT system rerandomized the subject to aprocitentan 25 mg or placebo in a 1:1 ratio. Rerandomization was stratified according to randomized treatment in DB Part 1 (which was not revealed). The IRT system assigned a scrambled, rerandomization number to the subject and bottle numbers which matched the treatment arm as assigned on the rerandomization list. At each subsequent visit when DB-WD treatment was dispensed, the IRT assigned the treatment bottle numbers according to the assigned treatment arm.
L1745: ## **Blinding and Unblinding**
L1746: ## **Blinding**
L1747: Two parts of the study were performed in a single-blind fashion: the placebo RI period prior to randomization, and SB Part 2. Study personnel were not permitted to inform subjects of the treatment received. Two parts were performed in a double-blind fashion: DB Part 1 and DB-WD Part 3. The Investigator and study personnel, subjects, CRAs, Sponsor personnel, and vendor/clinical research organization (CRO) personnel involved in the conduct of the study remained blinded to the treatment until database lock. Until the time of unblinding for final data analysis, the randomization/rerandomization lists were kept strictly confidential and were accessible only to Sponsor-authorized and CRO personnel who were not involved in the conduct of the study: IRT vendor personnel (responsible for clinical study supply distribution; subject level); the Sponsor's Pharmaceutical Development department personnel (clinical study supply distribution; depot level); the Sponsor's bioanalytical and biomarker laboratories; and the Independent Statistical Analysis Center and IDMC.
L1748: NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
L1749: The Sponsor notes that the study treatment was provided as tablets and that the investigational treatment and its matching placebo were indistinguishable, and all treatment bottles were packaged in the same way.
L1750: ## Unblinding
L1751: Final analyses: Randomization information was made available for data analysis only after database closure.
L1752: IDMC review: The Independent Statistical Analysis Center delivered unblinded data summaries to the IDMC for their review.
L1753: Serious, unexpected, and suspected adverse reaction (SUSAR): When a SUSAR occurred for a subject participating in the study, the Sponsor's Global Drug Safety department requested the unblinding of the treatment assignment. The treatment assignment was not communicated to site personnel, subjects, other Sponsor personnel, or any vendor/CRO personnel involved in the conduct of the study. Unblinded SUSAR information was provided to respective health authorities, in addition to Independent Ethics Committees/Institutional Review Boards (as per each committee's/board's specific requirements). SUSARs were reported to investigators in a blinded fashion.
L1754: Emergency: Emergency unblinding did not occur during this study.
L1755: ## Dosing
L1756: For Part 1 of the study, dosages of 12.5 and 25 mg once daily were administered. For Parts 2 and 3, the apocitentan dose administered was 25 mg once daily. Study treatment dose adjustments were not permitted. Study treatment could be temporarily interrupted in response to an AE, or other reasons (e.g., diagnostic or therapeutic procedure, a laboratory abnormality, study treatment forgotten). Interruptions of study treatment were no longer than 7 consecutive days during DB part and DB-WD part.
L1757: The decision to prematurely discontinue study treatment could be made by the subject, the Investigator, or Sponsor personnel. The main reason (e.g., AE, hypertension grade 3) was documented in the eCRF. Study-specific criteria for interruption / premature discontinuation of study treatment included hypertension (mean SiSBP \ge180 mmHg and/or mean SiDBP \ge110 mmHg measured by automated office blood pressure measurement and confirmed by repeat measurement), renal failure (confirmed eGFR &lt;15mL/min/1.73m2 or confirmed decrease of \ge 30% from baseline), persistent fluid retention (symptoms of fluid retention are not tolerable after increasing the dose of the existing diuretic or the addition of a new diuretic), hemoglobin abnormalities (interruption for hemoglobin decrease from baseline of &gt;20 g/L; discontinuation for decrease in hemoglobin to &lt;80 g/L, a decrease in hemoglobin from baseline of &gt;50 g/L, need for transfusion), pregnancy, and liver aminotransferases abnormalities (interruption for aminotransferases \ge3 and \le8 \times upper limit of normal (ULN); discontinuation for aminotransferases &gt;8 \times ULN, aminotransferases \ge3 \times ULN and associated clinical symptoms of liver injury, or aminotransferases \ge3 \times ULN and associated increase in total bilirubin \ge2 \times ULN).
L1758: ## Compliance
L1759: The study inclusion criteria include an adherence provision for randomization. Subjects could be enrolled if they demonstrated \ge 80\% compliance (pill counting) to study treatment (i.e., placebo) as well as \ge 80\% compliance (pill counting) to the standardized background antihypertensive therapy during the RI period. Adherence was defined as number of tablets dispensed at visit (n) minus number of tablets returned at Visit (n + 1) divided by the number of tablets that should have been taken during the period \times 100.
L1760: Between visits, adherence based on study treatment and the standardized background antihypertensive therapy accountability is expected to be at least 80%. Adherence values below 80% for the study treatment and/or the standardized background antihypertensive therapy without a medical justification (e.g., AE) after the randomization visit was considered a protocol deviation. In such cases, the Investigator was instructed to discuss and clarify the reasons for noncompliance with the subject and take appropriate actions to avoid re-occurrence. This discussion was to take place prior to providing the next study treatment to the subject.
L1761: Urine samples was collected throughout the study from initiation of the standardized background antihypertensive therapy and was analyzed for the presence of valsartan (one of the components of SBAT). The results of urine analysis from each visit were used to discuss the importance of study treatment intake with poorly adherent subjects.
L1762: ## Concomitant Medications
L1763: Treatments considered necessary for the subject's well-being and not categorized as forbidden concomitant medications were allowed during the study and must be documented in the medical charts.
L1764: The following therapies were allowed with the provision that they had been initiated and the dose stable at least 4 weeks prior to the screening visit and that the dose kept stable until the EOT: beta blockers, hormonal contraceptives, estrogen-replacement treatment, low dose acetylsalicylic acid for prevention of cardiovascular disease. In addition, the following therapies were allowed with the provision that they had been initiated at least 4 weeks prior to the screening visit and that the dose was kept stable until the end of DB part: nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs, selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors and anxiolytics.
L1765: Antihypertensive rescue medication was allowed in Part 2 and Part 3 according to local medical practice if, in the Investigator's opinion, the BP was not sufficiently controlled. Uncontrolled BP was confirmed via second measurement either at the next planned visit or at an unscheduled visit at the Investigator's discretion. In addition, adherence to the standardized background antihypertensive therapy was confirmed prior to initiating additional antihypertensive medication. Antihypertensive rescue medication was not allowed in the DB Part 1 of the study.
L1766: After Randomization, the dose of the existing diuretic (hydrochlorothiazide) could be increased, or a new diuretic could be added in the event of fluid retention as judged by the Investigator to be clinically relevant. The fluid retention was to be reported as an AE in the eCRF and fluid retention was to be reported as the indication for the diuretic. If the symptom(s) of fluid retention were not tolerable the subject was to discontinue the study treatment.
L1767: The following concomitant therapies were forbidden from the screening visit until the EOT visit (any drug which may affect BP, except rescue and allowed medications described above), ERAs, or other investigational drugs.
L1768: ## **Adverse Events of Special Interest**
L1769: Adverse events of special interest were categorized as: hepatic disorders, anemia, edema / fluid retention, and decompensation / aggravation of heart failure.
L1770: ## **Measures to Prevent Missing Data**
L1771: A subject who prematurely discontinues study treatment was not considered as withdrawn from the study, provided that the subject's consent for this limited participation in the study had not been withdrawn. If a randomized subject discontinued study treatment, the subject was asked to perform the EOT visit (i.e., Visit 15) as soon as possible after the last dose of study treatment but, in any case, no later than 7 days and a safety FU visit 30 (+3) days thereafter. The main reason (e.g., AE, hypertension grade 3) was documented in the eCRF. Antihypertensive medication(s) were administered according to local standard-of-care at the Investigator's discretion. The standardized background antihypertensive therapy could be continued until the completion of the safety FU visit.
L1772: A subject who prematurely discontinued study treatment at any stage and withdrew consent to participate in any further study assessments was considered as withdrawn from the study. Subjects who died or were lost to follow-up were also considered as withdrawn from the study.
L1773: Subjects were considered as lost to follow-up if all reasonable attempts by the Investigator to communicate with the individual had failed. The site was instructed to take preventive measures to avoid a subject being lost to follow-up (e.g., document different ways of contact such as telephone number, home address, email address, person to be contacted in the event the subject cannot be reached). If the subject could not be reached, the site was to make a reasonable effort to contact the subject, document all attempts (date, time and type of contact made), and enter the loss of follow-up information into the eCRF. The following methods were to be used: at least three attempts at contacts (e.g., telephone calls, or e-mails) must be placed to the last available telephone number or e-mail address, and one registered letter must be sent by post to the last available home address. Additional methods were considered acceptable if they were compliant with local rules/regulations (e.g., a visit by site personnel to the subject's home), respecting the subject's right to privacy. If the subject was still unreachable after all contact attempts listed above, he/she was considered to be lost to follow-up. The reason for premature withdrawal from the study was recorded in the eCRF.
L1774: If for whatever reason (except death or loss-to-follow-up) a subject was withdrawn from the study, the Investigator made his/her best efforts to schedule a last appointment / telephone call to assess the safety and well-being of the subject, collect unused study treatment and discuss follow-up medical care.
